# Command reference

Every NeptuneBASIC command, grouped by area. Generated by `python tools/gen_reference.py` from the command tables and [commands.txt](commands.txt); edit that file, not this one.

**Reading the entries:** a command that returns a value is shown as a function call, `Name(a, b)`, with the type it returns. Other commands are statements, `Name a, b`. A parameter's type is shown by the mark after its name: `%` a whole number, `#` a number with a fractional part, `$` text. Optional parameters are in brackets with their default value. **(ext)** marks commands NeptuneBASIC adds to the classic command set. Every entry has an example. To learn the language itself (`If`, `Type`, `Function`, ...), read [the manual](MANUAL.md).

1298 commands and 60 modern names.

## Program and system

### RuntimeError

`RuntimeError message$`

Stops the program at once with a runtime error showing message, plus the line and the chain of calls that led there. Use it for things that must never happen; nothing after it runs.

```basic
; stop with a message if a required value is missing
lives = 0
If lives <= 0 Then RuntimeError "The player has no lives left"
Print "never reached"
```

### CommandLine

`CommandLine()` → string

Returns the arguments passed to the program after the source or program name, joined by spaces.

```basic
; read the arguments given after the program name
args = CommandLine()
If args = "" Then Print "No arguments given" Else Print "Arguments: " + args
```

### GetEnv

`GetEnv(name$)` → string

Returns the value of the environment variable name, or an empty string if it is not set.

```basic
; read an environment variable (empty if not set)
user = GetEnv("USERNAME")
If user = "" Then Print "USERNAME is not set" Else Print "Hello, " + user
```

### SetEnv

`SetEnv name$, value$`

Sets the environment variable name to value for this program and any programs it starts.

```basic
; set a variable, then read it back
SetEnv "GAME_MODE", "hard"
Print GetEnv("GAME_MODE")   ; prints hard
```

### SystemProperty

`SystemProperty(property$)` → string

Returns a system property as a string: "appdir", "tempdir", "currentdir", "homedir", "windowsdir" and "systemdir" give folders ending in a path separator, "os" the operating system name and "cpucount" the number of CPU cores. Unknown properties return an empty string.

```basic
; ask the system about itself
Print "Operating system: " + SystemProperty("os")
Print "CPU cores: " + SystemProperty("cpucount")
Print "Temp folder: " + SystemProperty("tempdir")
```

### ExecFile

`ExecFile file$`

Opens file with the program the operating system associates with it (a document, web address or program); does not wait for it to finish.

```basic
; open a web address with the default browser (does not wait)
ExecFile "https://example.com"
Print "Browser requested"
```

## Console input and output

### Print

`Print [value=""]`

Writes value (any type, default an empty string) to the console followed by a new line.

```basic
; print text, numbers and an empty line
score = 120
Print "Score: " + score
Print 3.5
Print            ; an empty line
```

### Write

`Write value`

Writes value (any type) to the console without a new line.

```basic
; Write does not start a new line, so these join up
Write "Loading"
Write "..."
Print " done"
```

### Input

`Input([prompt$=""])` → string

Shows prompt on the console, waits for the user to type a line and returns it as a string without the line ending.

```basic
; ask the player a question and use the answer
name = Input("What is your name? ")
Print "Welcome, " + name
```

### DebugLog

`DebugLog message$`

Writes message to the debug log (standard error), followed by a new line.

```basic
; Debug messages go to the error stream (the console you launched from), not to the game window
health = 75
DebugLog "Player health is " + health
Print "This goes to the normal output"
```

## Maths

### Sqr

`Sqr(value#)` → float

Returns the square root of value as a float. A negative value gives NaN (not a number) instead of an error.

```basic
; distance between two points with Pythagoras
dx = 3.0
dy = 4.0
distance = Sqr(dx * dx + dy * dy)
Print distance   ; 5
```

### Sin

`Sin(degrees#)` → float

Returns the sine of degrees, an angle in degrees (not radians), as a float from -1 to 1. Sin(90) is 1.

```basic
; height of a bobbing object, angle in degrees
angle = 90
Print Sin(angle)   ; 1
Print Sin(0)       ; 0
```

### Cos

`Cos(degrees#)` → float

Returns the cosine of degrees, an angle in degrees (not radians), as a float from -1 to 1. Cos(0) is 1 and Cos(180) is -1.

```basic
; angles are in degrees
Print Cos(0)     ; 1
Print Cos(180)   ; -1
```

### Tan

`Tan(degrees#)` → float

Returns the tangent of degrees, an angle in degrees (not radians). Tan(45) is 1, and the result grows without limit as the angle nears 90 or 270.

```basic
; How steep is a ramp? Tan gives the rise for every 1 metre run (angles are in degrees)
angle = 30
Print "rise per metre: " + Tan(angle)   ; 0.57735
Print Tan(45)                           ; 1.0
```

### ASin

`ASin(value#)` → float

Returns the angle in degrees, from -90 to 90, whose sine is value. value must be from -1 to 1: anything else gives NaN (not a number).

```basic
; the angle whose sine is 0.5
angle = ASin(0.5)
Print angle   ; about 30 (floats are approximate)
```

### ACos

`ACos(value#)` → float

Returns the angle in degrees, from 0 to 180, whose cosine is value. value must be from -1 to 1: anything else gives NaN (not a number).

```basic
; the angle whose cosine is 0.5
angle = ACos(0.5)
Print angle   ; about 60
```

### ATan

`ATan(value#)` → float

Returns the angle in degrees, from -90 to 90, whose tangent is value. To get a full-circle angle from a distance across and up, use ATan2 instead.

```basic
; the angle whose tangent is 1
angle = ATan(1)
Print angle   ; 45
```

### ATan2

`ATan2(y#, x#)` → float

Returns the angle in degrees of the point (x, y) from the origin, from -180 to 180; note the y parameter comes first.

```basic
; direction from the origin to the point (x=1, y=1); y comes first
angle = ATan2(1, 1)
Print angle   ; 45
```

### Exp

`Exp(value#)` → float

Returns e (about 2.718282) raised to the power value, so Exp(0) is 1. It is the opposite of Log.

```basic
; Fog gets thicker with distance: this is the fraction of light left after 10 metres
distance# = 10
density# = 0.1
visible# = Exp(-density * distance)
Print visible   ; 0.367879
```

### Log

`Log(value#)` → float

Returns the natural logarithm of value, the opposite of Exp. Log(0) is -Infinity and a negative value gives NaN. For another base, divide: Log(x) / Log(base).

```basic
; How many times must you double 1 to pass 1000? Log works out the power
doublings# = Log(1000) / Log(2)
Print doublings   ; 9.965784
```

### Log10

`Log10(value#)` → float

Returns the base-10 logarithm of value: Log10(1000) is 3. Log10(0) is -Infinity and a negative value gives NaN.

```basic
; Count the digits of a score: 98765 has a base-10 logarithm of about 4.99.
; Floor drops the .99 (Int would round it up to 5).
score = 98765
digits = Floor(Log10(score)) + 1
Print digits   ; 5
```

### Floor

`Floor(value#)` → float

Returns value rounded down to the nearest whole number, as a float.

```basic
; round down to a whole number (still a float)
Print Floor(3.7)    ; 3.0
Print Floor(-3.2)   ; -4.0
```

### Ceil

`Ceil(value#)` → float

Returns value rounded up to the nearest whole number, as a float.

```basic
; round up to a whole number (still a float)
Print Ceil(3.2)    ; 4.0
Print Ceil(-3.7)   ; -3.0
```

### Min

`Min(a#, b#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the smaller of a and b. The result is a float, so storing it in an integer variable rounds it.

```basic
; the smaller of two values
damage = Min(50, 20)
Print damage   ; 20
```

### Max

`Max(a#, b#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the larger of a and b. The result is a float, so storing it in an integer variable rounds it.

```basic
; the larger of two values
health = Max(0, -5)
Print health   ; 0
```

### Clamp

`Clamp(value#, low#, high#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns value limited to the range low to high: low if value is below it, high if value is above it, otherwise value unchanged. The result is a float.

```basic
; keep a value between 0 and 100
health = Clamp(130, 0, 100)
Print health   ; 100
```

### Lerp

`Lerp(a#, b#, t#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the value a fraction t of the way from a to b (t = 0 gives a, t = 1 gives b).

```basic
; halfway between 10 and 20
x = Lerp(10, 20, 0.5)
Print x   ; 15
```

### Ease

`Ease(t#, [ease%=3])` → float

**(ext)** Returns how far along an easing curve is at time t (0 start, 1 end): EASE_LINEAR, EASE_IN (starts slow), EASE_OUT (ends slow), EASE_IN_OUT (the default), EASE_IN_CUBIC, EASE_OUT_CUBIC, EASE_IN_OUT_CUBIC, EASE_IN_SINE, EASE_OUT_SINE, EASE_IN_OUT_SINE, EASE_IN_BACK, EASE_OUT_BACK, EASE_IN_OUT_BACK (overshoot a little), EASE_IN_ELASTIC, EASE_OUT_ELASTIC (wobble), EASE_IN_BOUNCE, EASE_OUT_BOUNCE. Use it to move anything smoothly.

```basic
; a box that glides in from the left and settles
For frame = 0 To 60
    x# = -200 + 400 * Ease(frame / 60.0, EASE_OUT_CUBIC)
Next
```

### EaseBetween

`EaseBetween(from#, to#, t#, [ease%=3])` → float

**(ext)** Returns the value between from and to at time t (0-1) along easing curve ease, for smooth movement of menus, counters and anything else.

```basic
; a score counter that rolls up from 0 to 1500 in one second
shown = EaseBetween(0, 1500, 0.5, EASE_OUT)
Print shown
```

## Random numbers

### SeedRnd

`SeedRnd seed%`

Seeds the random number generator with seed so the same sequence of numbers is produced each run; MilliSecs() is a common seed.

```basic
; the same seed always gives the same random numbers
SeedRnd 1234
roll_a = Rand(1, 100)
SeedRnd 1234
roll_b = Rand(1, 100)
Print roll_a = roll_b   ; 1 (true)
```

### Rnd

`Rnd(from#, [to#=0])` → float

Returns a random float between from and to; with only one parameter it returns a float between 0 and from.

```basic
; a random float between 0 and 1, and between -5 and 5
SeedRnd MilliSecs()
chance = Rnd(1)
offset = Rnd(-5, 5)
Print chance
Print offset
```

### Rand

`Rand(from%, [to%=1])` → integer

Returns a random integer between from and to inclusive; with only one parameter it returns an integer between 1 and from.

```basic
; roll a six-sided die
SeedRnd MilliSecs()
roll = Rand(6)
Print "You rolled " + roll
Print "Between 10 and 20: " + Rand(10, 20)
```

### RndSeed

`RndSeed()` → integer

Returns the seed last passed to SeedRnd (0 if never called). It does not change as you draw random numbers, so save it to replay the same sequence.

```basic
; read back the seed that was set
SeedRnd 42
Print RndSeed()   ; 42
```

## Strings

### Left

`Left(string$, count%)` → string

Returns the first count characters of string. If count is larger than the string, the whole string comes back; 0 or less gives an empty string.

```basic
; the first 3 characters
word = "Neptune"
Print Left(word, 3)   ; Nep
```

### Right

`Right(string$, count%)` → string

Returns the last count characters of string. If count is larger than the string, the whole string comes back; 0 or less gives an empty string.

```basic
; the last 4 characters
word = "Neptune"
Print Right(word, 4)   ; tune
```

### Mid

`Mid(string$, offset%, [count%=-1])` → string

Returns count characters of string starting at character offset (1 is the first character); with count left at -1 it returns the rest of the string.

```basic
; 3 characters starting at character 2, and the rest of the text
word = "Neptune"
Print Mid(word, 2, 3)   ; ept
Print Mid(word, 4)      ; tune
```

### Len

`Len(string$)` → integer

Returns the number of characters in string, which is 0 for an empty string. Spaces and punctuation count, and a letter with an accent counts as one.

```basic
; count the characters
name = "Neptune"
Print Len(name)   ; 7
```

### Upper

`Upper(string$)` → string

Returns string with every letter in capitals. The original is not changed, so assign the result to keep it. Use it for headings, or on both sides to compare text ignoring case.

```basic
; capitalise the first letter of a name typed in lower case
name$ = "neptune"
Print Upper(Left(name$, 1)) + Mid(name$, 2)   ; Neptune
Print Upper("game over")   ; GAME OVER
```

### Lower

`Lower(string$)` → string

Returns string with every letter in lower case. The original is not changed. Use it, or Upper, on both sides when comparing player input so "Yes" and "YES" match.

```basic
; compare text without caring about case
answer = "YES"
If Lower(answer) = "yes" Then Print "Confirmed"
```

### Trim

`Trim(string$)` → string

Returns string with spaces and other white space removed from both ends.

```basic
; remove spaces from both ends
name = Trim("   Bob  ")
Print "[" + name + "]"   ; [Bob]
```

### LSet

`LSet(string$, size%)` → string

Returns string padded with spaces on the right, or cut, to exactly size characters.

```basic
; pad on the right to make columns line up
Print LSet("Name", 10) + "|"    ; Name      |
Print LSet("Neptune", 4) + "|"  ; Nept|
```

### RSet

`RSet(string$, size%)` → string

Returns string padded with spaces on the left, or cut from the left, to exactly size characters.

```basic
; pad on the left to right-align numbers
Print RSet("42", 6)          ; "    42"
Print RSet("123456789", 4)   ; 6789
```

### Instr

`Instr(string$, find$, [from%=1])` → integer

Returns the position of find in string, searching from character from (default 1), or 0 if it is not found.

```basic
; find where text appears (0 if it does not)
sentence = "the quick brown fox"
pos = Instr(sentence, "quick")
Print pos   ; 5
If Instr(sentence, "cat") = 0 Then Print "no cat"
```

### Replace

`Replace(string$, from$, to$)` → string

Returns string with every occurrence of from replaced by to.

```basic
; swap every occurrence of one piece of text for another
text = "red car, red house"
Print Replace(text, "red", "blue")   ; blue car, blue house
```

### String

`String(string$, count%)` → string

Returns string repeated count times, so String("-", 20) is a line of 20 dashes. A count of 0 or less gives an empty string. Handy for bars, padding and borders.

```basic
; repeat text to draw a bar
health = 7
Print "[" + String("#", health) + "]"
```

### Chr

`Chr(code%)` → string

Returns the one-character string whose character code is code (65 is "A", 32 is a space). Asc does the reverse. Codes that are not valid characters give an empty string.

```basic
; character code 65 is the letter A
letter = Chr(65)
Print letter   ; A
```

### Asc

`Asc(string$)` → integer

Returns the character code of the first character of string, or -1 for an empty string.

```basic
; the character code of the first letter
code = Asc("A")
Print code   ; 65
```

### Hex

`Hex(value%)` → string

Returns value as exactly 8 hexadecimal digits in capitals, with leading zeros, such as 000000FF for 255. Negative numbers show their 32-bit form, so Hex(-1) is FFFFFFFF.

```basic
; show a number as 8 hex digits
Print Hex(255)        ; 000000FF
Print Hex(16711680)   ; 00FF0000
```

### Bin

`Bin(value%)` → string

Returns value as exactly 32 binary digits (0 and 1) with leading zeros, which makes bit flags easy to see. Negative numbers show their 32-bit form, so Bin(-1) is all 1s.

```basic
; test which of 8 flag bits are set in a number
flags = 5
Print Right(Bin(flags), 8)   ; 00000101
Print "Bit 0 set: " + ((flags And 1) <> 0)   ; 1
Print "Bit 1 set: " + ((flags And 2) <> 0)   ; 0
```

### TypeOf

`TypeOf(value)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of the type of value as a string, such as "Int", "Float" or "String".

```basic
; find out what kind of value a variable holds
score = 10
speed = 2.5
name = "Bob"
Print TypeOf(score)   ; Int
Print TypeOf(speed)   ; Float
Print TypeOf(name)    ; String
```

### FormatFloat

`FormatFloat(value#, decimals%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns value as a string with decimals digits after the decimal point (0 to 9).

```basic
; show a number with exactly 2 decimals
price = 3.14159
Print FormatFloat(price, 2)   ; 3.14
```

## Files and folders

### FileType

`FileType(path$)` → integer

Returns 1 if path is a file, 2 if it is a folder, or 0 if it does not exist.

```basic
; 0 = does not exist, 1 = file, 2 = folder
kind = FileType("save.dat")
If kind = 0 Then Print "No save file"
If kind = 1 Then Print "Found a file"
If kind = 2 Then Print "That is a folder"
```

### FileSize

`FileSize(path$)` → integer

Returns the size of the file at path in bytes, or 0 if it does not exist.

```basic
; write a small file and measure it
file = WriteFile("size_test.txt")
WriteLine file, "hello"
CloseFile file
Print FileSize("size_test.txt") + " bytes"
DeleteFile "size_test.txt"
```

### DeleteFile

`DeleteFile path$`

Deletes the file at path. Nothing happens, and no error is raised, if the file does not exist or cannot be deleted. Close any stream open on the file first.

```basic
; remove a file if it exists
file = WriteFile("old_save.dat")
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "old_save.dat"
Print FileType("old_save.dat")   ; 0
```

### CopyFile

`CopyFile from$, to$`

Copies the file from to the path to, replacing to if it already exists. Nothing happens, and no error is raised, if from is missing; check with FileType if it matters.

```basic
; make a backup of a save file
file = WriteFile("save.dat")
WriteInt file, 100
CloseFile file
CopyFile "save.dat", "save_backup.dat"
Print FileType("save_backup.dat")   ; 1
DeleteFile "save.dat"
DeleteFile "save_backup.dat"
```

### CreateDir

`CreateDir path$`

Creates the folder path, along with any missing folders above it. It is harmless if the folder already exists, and nothing is reported if it cannot be made, so check with FileType.

```basic
; make a folder for save games
CreateDir "saves"
If FileType("saves") = 2 Then Print "Folder ready"
DeleteDir "saves"
```

### DeleteDir

`DeleteDir path$`

Deletes the folder path, which must be empty. If it still holds files, or does not exist, nothing happens and no error is raised. Delete its files first with DeleteFile.

```basic
; a folder must be empty before it can be deleted
CreateDir "temp_stuff"
DeleteDir "temp_stuff"
Print FileType("temp_stuff")   ; 0
```

### CurrentDir

`CurrentDir()` → string

Returns the full path of the folder the program is working in, ending in a path separator so a file name can be added straight on. Relative file names are looked up here.

```basic
; build the full path of a save file in the working folder
path$ = CurrentDir() + "save.dat"
Print "Saving to " + path$
file = WriteFile(path$)
WriteInt file, 100
CloseFile file
Print FileType(path$)   ; 1
DeleteFile path$
```

### ChangeDir

`ChangeDir path$`

Makes path the current folder, so relative file names are found there from now on. If the folder does not exist nothing happens and no error is raised. CurrentDir tells you where you are.

```basic
; move into a folder, then back again
start = CurrentDir()
CreateDir "levels"
ChangeDir "levels"
Print CurrentDir()
ChangeDir start
DeleteDir "levels"
```

### ReadDir

`ReadDir(dirname$)` → integer

Opens the folder dirname for listing and returns a handle, or 0 if it cannot be opened.

```basic
; list everything in the current folder
dir = ReadDir(".")
If dir <> 0
    name = NextFile(dir)
    While name <> ""
        Print name
        name = NextFile(dir)
    Wend
    CloseDir dir
EndIf
```

### NextFile

`NextFile(dir%)` → string

Returns the next name in the folder listing dir ("." and ".." come first), or an empty string when there are no more; use FileType to tell files from folders.

```basic
; list the files (not the folders) of the current folder
dir = ReadDir(".")
name = NextFile(dir)
While name <> ""
    If FileType(name) = 1 Then Print name
    name = NextFile(dir)
Wend
CloseDir dir
```

### CloseDir

`CloseDir dir%`

Closes the folder listing dir from ReadDir, which cannot be used with NextFile afterwards. Close every listing once you have read the names you need.

```basic
; list the files in a folder, then close the listing
CreateDir "demo_files"
file = WriteFile("demo_files/a.txt") : CloseFile file
dir = ReadDir("demo_files")
name$ = NextFile(dir)
While name$ <> ""
    Print name$    ; ".", "..", then "a.txt"
    name$ = NextFile(dir)
Wend
CloseDir dir
DeleteFile "demo_files/a.txt"
DeleteDir "demo_files"
```

## Settings and save data

### UserFolder

`UserFolder([app$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns the folder (made if needed) where a game should keep its settings and saves: the user's application data folder with a folder named app in it (default "NeptuneBASIC"), ending in a path separator. It is writable on every system, unlike the game's own folder.

```basic
; Keep saves in the player's own application folder, which is always writable.
; The result ends with a slash, so add the file name straight on.
folder$ = UserFolder("My Space Game")
SetSetting "best.score", "1200"
SaveSettings folder$ + "settings.txt"
```

### SetSetting

`SetSetting name$, value$`

**(ext)** Stores value as the setting called name (in memory; SaveSettings writes them all to a file). Names are not case sensitive and may use dots to group them, like "audio.music" or "slot1.level".

```basic
SetSetting "audio.music", 0.8
SetSetting "player.name", "Ann"
SetSetting "best.score", 12500
```

### GetSetting

`GetSetting(name$, [default$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns the setting called name, or default if there is none.

```basic
name$ = GetSetting("player.name", "Player")
Print "Hello " + name
```

### GetSettingInt

`GetSettingInt(name$, [default%=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the setting called name as a whole number, or default if there is none or it is not a number.

```basic
; Show the best score, or 0 when no score has been saved yet
SetSetting "best.score", "1200"   ; normally this comes from LoadSettings
Print GetSettingInt("best.score", 0)    ; 1200
Print GetSettingInt("best.time", -1)    ; -1 (no such setting, so the default)
```

### GetSettingFloat

`GetSettingFloat(name$, [default#=0])` → float

**(ext)** Returns the setting called name as a number, or default if there is none or it is not a number.

```basic
; Music volume from the options, falling back to full volume the first time
SetSetting "audio.music", "0.75"
Print GetSettingFloat("audio.music", 1.0)    ; 0.75
Print GetSettingFloat("audio.effects", 1.0)  ; 1.0
```

### SettingExists

`SettingExists(name$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 if there is a setting called name (even if its value is empty), else 0. Names ignore upper and lower case, so "Slot1.Level" and "slot1.level" are the same setting.

```basic
; Offer "Continue" only when a game has been saved
SetSetting "slot1.level", "4"
If SettingExists("slot1.level") Then Print "Continue?"   ; Continue?
If Not SettingExists("slot2.level") Then Print "Slot 2 is empty"   ; Slot 2 is empty
```

### DeleteSetting

`DeleteSetting name$`

**(ext)** Removes the setting called name from memory; nothing happens if there is none. A saved file only changes when you call SaveSettings again.

```basic
; Empty a save slot
SetSetting "slot1.level", "4"
DeleteSetting "slot1.level"
Print SettingExists("slot1.level")   ; 0
```

### ClearSettings

`ClearSettings`

**(ext)** Removes every setting from memory, for example before LoadSettings to start from nothing. A saved file is not touched until you call SaveSettings.

```basic
; Reset to the defaults (the saved file is untouched until SaveSettings is called)
SetSetting "audio.music", "0.2"
ClearSettings
Print GetSettingFloat("audio.music", 1.0)   ; 1.0
```

### SaveSettings

`SaveSettings file$`

**(ext)** Writes every setting to file as name = value lines, a text file people can read and edit.

```basic
; Save the options to a text file the player can read and edit
SetSetting "fullscreen", "1"
SaveSettings "settings.txt"   ; in a real game: UserFolder("My Game") + "settings.txt"
f = ReadFile("settings.txt")
Print ReadLine(f)   ; fullscreen = 1
CloseFile f
DeleteFile "settings.txt"
```

### LoadSettings

`LoadSettings(file$)` → integer

**(ext)** Reads settings from file (adding to or replacing those in memory) and returns 1, or 0 if the file does not exist.

```basic
; Read the options at start-up. The first run has no file, so keep the defaults
SetSetting "fullscreen", "0"
SaveSettings "settings.txt"
ClearSettings
If LoadSettings("settings.txt") Then Print "loaded"   ; loaded
DeleteFile "settings.txt"
If LoadSettings("settings.txt") = 0 Then Print "first run"   ; first run
```

## Streams

### ReadFile

`ReadFile(path$)` → integer

Opens the file at path for reading and returns a stream handle, or 0 if it cannot be opened.

```basic
; read a text file line by line
file = ReadFile("highscores.txt")
If file = 0
    Print "Could not open the file"
Else
    While Not Eof(file)
        Print ReadLine(file)
    Wend
    CloseFile file
EndIf
```

### WriteFile

`WriteFile(path$)` → integer

Creates (or empties) the file at path for writing and returns a stream handle, or 0 on failure.

```basic
; create a text file and write two lines
file = WriteFile("notes.txt")
If file <> 0
    WriteLine file, "First line"
    WriteLine file, "Second line"
    CloseFile file
EndIf
```

### OpenFile

`OpenFile(path$)` → integer

Opens an existing file at path for reading and writing and returns a stream handle, or 0 on failure.

```basic
; open an existing file to read and change it
file = OpenFile("save.dat")
If file <> 0
    Print "Old value: " + ReadInt(file)
    SeekFile file, 0       ; go back to the start
    WriteInt file, 200     ; overwrite it
    CloseFile file
EndIf
```

### CloseFile

`CloseFile stream%`

Closes stream, whatever opened it (a file or a network connection). Data written to a file is saved by then. The handle is invalid afterwards; closing a handle that is already closed does nothing.

```basic
; close the file so everything is saved
file = WriteFile("log.txt")
WriteLine file, "Game started"
CloseFile file
```

### Eof

`Eof(stream%)` → integer

Returns true once stream has no more data to read (for a TCP stream, when the connection has closed).

```basic
; keep reading until the end of the file
file = ReadFile("names.txt")
If file <> 0
    While Not Eof(file)
        Print ReadLine(file)
    Wend
    CloseFile file
EndIf
```

### ReadLine

`ReadLine(stream%)` → string

Reads a line of text from stream and returns it without the line ending.

```basic
; read the first line of a file
file = ReadFile("names.txt")
If file <> 0
    firstname = ReadLine(file)
    Print "First name: " + firstname
    CloseFile file
EndIf
```

### WriteLine

`WriteLine stream%, text$`

Writes text to stream followed by a Windows line ending (carriage return and line feed), the same on every system. ReadLine takes the line ending off again when reading it back.

```basic
; write a line of text to a file
file = WriteFile("log.txt")
WriteLine file, "Player entered level 1"
CloseFile file
```

### ReadByte

`ReadByte(stream%)` → integer

Reads one byte (0-255) from stream and returns it, moving on one byte. At the end of the file it returns 0 instead of failing: check Eof first.

```basic
; read a single byte (0 to 255)
file = WriteFile("byte.dat")
WriteByte file, 200
CloseFile file
file = ReadFile("byte.dat")
Print ReadByte(file)   ; 200
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "byte.dat"
```

### ReadShort

`ReadShort(stream%)` → integer

Reads a 2-byte unsigned integer (0-65535) from stream and returns it.

```basic
; read a 2-byte number (0 to 65535)
file = WriteFile("short.dat")
WriteShort file, 40000
CloseFile file
file = ReadFile("short.dat")
Print ReadShort(file)   ; 40000
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "short.dat"
```

### ReadInt

`ReadInt(stream%)` → integer

Reads a 4-byte integer (about -2.1 billion to 2.1 billion) from stream and returns it, moving on four bytes. Past the end of the file it returns 0: check Eof first.

```basic
; read a 4-byte whole number
file = WriteFile("score.dat")
WriteInt file, 123456
CloseFile file
file = ReadFile("score.dat")
score = ReadInt(file)
Print score   ; 123456
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "score.dat"
```

### ReadFloat

`ReadFloat(stream%)` → float

Reads a 4-byte float from stream and returns it, moving on four bytes. It has about 7 digits of accuracy. Past the end of the file it returns 0: check Eof first.

```basic
; read a 4-byte decimal number
file = WriteFile("speed.dat")
WriteFloat file, 2.5
CloseFile file
file = ReadFile("speed.dat")
speed = ReadFloat(file)
Print speed   ; 2.5
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "speed.dat"
```

### ReadString

`ReadString(stream%)` → string

Reads a string stored as a 4-byte length followed by its characters (as written by WriteString) from stream.

```basic
; read text saved with WriteString
file = WriteFile("name.dat")
WriteString file, "Captain"
CloseFile file
file = ReadFile("name.dat")
name = ReadString(file)
Print name   ; Captain
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "name.dat"
```

### WriteByte

`WriteByte stream%, value%`

Writes value to stream as one byte. Only the low 8 bits are kept, so use 0-255 (256 is stored as 0). Read it back with ReadByte.

```basic
; save a value from 0 to 255
file = WriteFile("level.dat")
WriteByte file, 7
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "level.dat"
```

### WriteShort

`WriteShort stream%, value%`

Writes value to stream as a 2-byte integer. Only the low 16 bits are kept, so use 0-65535 (65536 is stored as 0). Read it back with ReadShort.

```basic
; save a value from 0 to 65535
file = WriteFile("coins.dat")
WriteShort file, 1500
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "coins.dat"
```

### WriteInt

`WriteInt stream%, value%`

Writes value to stream as a 4-byte integer, which holds whole numbers from about -2.1 billion to 2.1 billion. Read it back with ReadInt.

```basic
; save a whole number
file = WriteFile("score.dat")
WriteInt file, 99999
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "score.dat"
```

### WriteFloat

`WriteFloat stream%, value#`

Writes value to stream as a 4-byte float, which keeps about 7 digits, so a very precise number comes back slightly rounded. Read it back with ReadFloat.

```basic
; save a decimal number
file = WriteFile("position.dat")
WriteFloat file, 12.75
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "position.dat"
```

### WriteString

`WriteString stream%, value$`

Writes value to stream as a 4-byte length followed by its characters.

```basic
; save text with its length, so ReadString can read it back
file = WriteFile("player.dat")
WriteString file, "Captain"
WriteInt file, 3
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "player.dat"
```

### ReadAvail

`ReadAvail(stream%)` → integer

Returns how many bytes can be read from stream without waiting: for a file, the bytes left before its end.

```basic
; how many bytes are left to read
file = WriteFile("data.dat")
WriteInt file, 1
WriteInt file, 2
CloseFile file
file = ReadFile("data.dat")
Print ReadAvail(file)   ; 8
skipped = ReadInt(file)
Print ReadAvail(file)   ; 4
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "data.dat"
```

### FilePos

`FilePos(stream%)` → integer

Returns the current read/write position in the file stream, in bytes from the start.

```basic
; see how far through the file we are
file = WriteFile("pos.dat")
WriteInt file, 10
Print FilePos(file)   ; 4
WriteInt file, 20
Print FilePos(file)   ; 8
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "pos.dat"
```

### SeekFile

`SeekFile(stream%, pos%)` → integer

Moves the read/write position of the file stream to pos bytes from the start and returns the new position.

```basic
; jump to the second number in a file
file = WriteFile("numbers.dat")
WriteInt file, 10
WriteInt file, 20
CloseFile file
file = OpenFile("numbers.dat")
newpos = SeekFile(file, 4)   ; skip the first 4 bytes
Print ReadInt(file)          ; 20
CloseFile file
DeleteFile "numbers.dat"
```

### CopyStream

`CopyStream src_stream%, dest_stream%, [buffer_size%=16384]`

Copies all remaining data from src_stream to dest_stream, buffer_size bytes at a time.

```basic
; copy one file to another using streams
source = ReadFile("level1.dat")
If source <> 0
    copy = WriteFile("level1_copy.dat")
    CopyStream source, copy    ; default buffer size
    CloseFile copy
    CloseFile source
EndIf
```

## Banks

### CreateBank

`CreateBank([size%=0])` → integer

Creates a bank (a block of memory) of size bytes, filled with zeros, and returns its handle.

```basic
; make a bank of 16 bytes (filled with zeros)
bank = CreateBank(16)
Print BankSize(bank)   ; 16
FreeBank bank
```

### FreeBank

`FreeBank bank%`

Frees bank and its memory. The handle is invalid afterwards, and using it with BankSize, PeekByte, PokeByte and the like is a runtime error.

```basic
; Make a scratch bank, use it, then free it. Its handle is invalid afterwards.
bank = CreateBank(1024)
PokeInt bank, 0, 42
Print PeekInt(bank, 0)   ; 42
FreeBank bank
```

### BankSize

`BankSize(bank%)` → integer

Returns the size of bank in bytes: the size given to CreateBank, or the newest size from ResizeBank.

```basic
; ask how big a bank is
bank = CreateBank(256)
Print "Bank holds " + BankSize(bank) + " bytes"
FreeBank bank
```

### ResizeBank

`ResizeBank bank%, size%`

Changes the size of bank to size bytes, keeping its contents; new bytes are zero.

```basic
; grow a bank; the old data is kept and new bytes are zero
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeInt bank, 0, 77
ResizeBank bank, 8
Print PeekInt(bank, 0)   ; 77
Print PeekInt(bank, 4)   ; 0
FreeBank bank
```

### CopyBank

`CopyBank src_bank%, src_offset%, dest_bank%, dest_offset%, count%`

Copies count bytes from src_bank at src_offset to dest_bank at dest_offset.

```basic
; copy 4 bytes from one bank into another
source = CreateBank(8)
target = CreateBank(8)
PokeInt source, 0, 1234
CopyBank source, 0, target, 4, 4   ; from source at 0 to target at 4, 4 bytes
Print PeekInt(target, 4)           ; 1234
FreeBank source
FreeBank target
```

### PeekByte

`PeekByte(bank%, offset%)` → integer

Returns the byte (0-255) at offset in bank, where 0 is the first byte. An offset outside the bank is a runtime error.

```basic
; read one byte from a bank
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeByte bank, 2, 200
Print PeekByte(bank, 2)   ; 200
FreeBank bank
```

### PeekShort

`PeekShort(bank%, offset%)` → integer

Returns the unsigned 2-byte integer (0-65535) at offset in bank, using the bytes offset and offset + 1. Both must be inside the bank or it is a runtime error.

```basic
; read a 2-byte number from a bank
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeShort bank, 0, 5000
Print PeekShort(bank, 0)   ; 5000
FreeBank bank
```

### PeekInt

`PeekInt(bank%, offset%)` → integer

Returns the 4-byte integer at offset in bank, using the bytes from offset to offset + 3. All four must be inside the bank or it is a runtime error.

```basic
; read a 4-byte number from a bank
bank = CreateBank(8)
PokeInt bank, 4, 100000
Print PeekInt(bank, 4)   ; 100000
FreeBank bank
```

### PeekFloat

`PeekFloat(bank%, offset%)` → float

Returns the 4-byte float at offset in bank, using the bytes from offset to offset + 3. It has about 7 digits of accuracy. All four must be inside the bank or it is a runtime error.

```basic
; read a 4-byte decimal from a bank
bank = CreateBank(8)
PokeFloat bank, 0, 1.5
Print PeekFloat(bank, 0)   ; 1.5
FreeBank bank
```

### PokeByte

`PokeByte bank%, offset%, value%`

Writes value as one byte at offset in bank. Only the low 8 bits are kept, so use 0-255 (256 is stored as 0). An offset outside the bank is a runtime error.

```basic
; store a value from 0 to 255 at offset 0
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeByte bank, 0, 255
Print PeekByte(bank, 0)
FreeBank bank
```

### PokeShort

`PokeShort bank%, offset%, value%`

Writes value as a 2-byte integer at offset in bank.

```basic
; store a 2-byte number at offset 2
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeShort bank, 2, 60000
Print PeekShort(bank, 2)
FreeBank bank
```

### PokeInt

`PokeInt bank%, offset%, value%`

Writes value as a 4-byte integer at offset in bank.

```basic
; store a 4-byte number at offset 0
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeInt bank, 0, -5
Print PeekInt(bank, 0)   ; -5
FreeBank bank
```

### PokeFloat

`PokeFloat bank%, offset%, value#`

Writes value as a 4-byte float at offset in bank.

```basic
; store a decimal number at offset 4
bank = CreateBank(8)
PokeFloat bank, 4, 3.25
Print PeekFloat(bank, 4)   ; 3.25
FreeBank bank
```

### ReadBytes

`ReadBytes(bank%, stream%, offset%, count%)` → integer

Reads up to count bytes from stream into bank starting at offset and returns the number of bytes read.

```basic
; load the bytes of a file into a bank
file = ReadFile("data.bin")
If file <> 0
    bank = CreateBank(64)
    count = ReadBytes(bank, file, 0, 64)   ; up to 64 bytes, into the bank from offset 0
    Print count + " bytes read"
    CloseFile file
    FreeBank bank
EndIf
```

### WriteBytes

`WriteBytes(bank%, stream%, offset%, count%)` → integer

Writes count bytes from bank starting at offset to stream and returns the number written.

```basic
; save the contents of a bank to a file
bank = CreateBank(4)
PokeInt bank, 0, 42
file = WriteFile("bank.dat")
written = WriteBytes(bank, file, 0, 4)   ; 4 bytes from bank offset 0
Print written + " bytes written"
CloseFile file
FreeBank bank
DeleteFile "bank.dat"
```

## Time and timers

### MilliSecs

`MilliSecs()` → integer

Returns the number of milliseconds since the program started. Subtract two readings to time something; the value wraps around after about 24 days.

```basic
; measure how long something takes
start = MilliSecs()
Delay 50
elapsed = MilliSecs() - start
Print "Took " + elapsed + " ms"
```

### Delay

`Delay millisecs%`

Pauses the program for millisecs milliseconds (negative counts as 0). Nothing else happens meanwhile, not even redrawing, so keep delays short; for a steady frame rate use CreateTimer and WaitTimer instead.

```basic
; pause for half a second
Print "Wait..."
Delay 500
Print "Done"
```

### CurrentDate

`CurrentDate()` → string

Returns today's date as a string in the form "dd Mon yyyy".

```basic
; put today's date in a save file name, for example 07 Nov 2026
today$ = CurrentDate()
Print "Saved on " + today
Print Len(today)        ; 11
```

### CurrentTime

`CurrentTime()` → string

Returns the current time as a string in the form "hh:mm:ss".

```basic
; stamp a log line with the time, for example 14:30:05
stamp$ = CurrentTime()
Print "[" + stamp + "] Game started"
Print Len(stamp)        ; 8
```

### CreateTimer

`CreateTimer(hertz%)` → integer

Creates a timer that ticks hertz times per second and returns its handle; use it with WaitTimer to run a program at a steady frame rate.

```basic
; keep a loop at 60 steps per second with CreateTimer and WaitTimer
timer = CreateTimer(60)
For i = 1 To 3
    ticks = WaitTimer(timer)
    Print "Frame " + i
Next
FreeTimer timer
```

### WaitTimer

`WaitTimer(timer%)` → integer

Waits until timer next ticks and returns the number of ticks that have passed since the last call (at least 1).

```basic
; run a loop at a steady 60 steps per second
timer = CreateTimer(60)
For frame = 1 To 3
    ticks = WaitTimer(timer)   ; at least 1; more if the program fell behind
    Print "Step " + frame + ", ticks passed: " + ticks
Next
FreeTimer timer
```

### FreeTimer

`FreeTimer timer%`

Deletes timer, which stops it ticking. Its handle is invalid afterwards, and WaitTimer on it gives an error.

```basic
; delete the timer when finished with it
timer = CreateTimer(30)
ticks = WaitTimer(timer)
FreeTimer timer
```

## Networking

### OpenTCPStream

`OpenTCPStream(server$, port%)` → integer

Connects to server (a host name or IP address) on port and returns a TCP stream handle, or 0 if the connection fails. Use the stream commands to send and receive.

```basic
; connect to a server and send a line
stream = OpenTCPStream("127.0.0.1", 5000)
If stream = 0
    Print "Could not connect"
Else
    WriteLine stream, "hello"
    CloseTCPStream stream
EndIf
```

### CloseTCPStream

`CloseTCPStream stream%`

Closes the TCP stream and ends the connection. Its handle is invalid afterwards. Closing a stream that is already closed does nothing.

```basic
; talk over a local connection, then close both ends
server = CreateTCPServer(47123)
client = OpenTCPStream("127.0.0.1", 47123)
conn = AcceptTCPStream(server)
WriteLine client, "hello"
Print ReadLine(conn)        ; hello
CloseTCPStream client
CloseTCPStream conn
CloseTCPServer server
```

### CreateTCPServer

`CreateTCPServer(port%)` → integer

Starts a TCP server listening on port and returns its handle, or 0 on failure.

```basic
; start listening on port 5000
server = CreateTCPServer(5000)
If server = 0 Then Print "Could not start the server" Else Print "Listening"
If server <> 0 Then CloseTCPServer server
```

### CloseTCPServer

`CloseTCPServer server%`

Stops the TCP server listening on its port, so nobody new can connect. Streams already accepted from it stay open and must be closed with CloseTCPStream.

```basic
; stop listening; a stream already accepted stays open until it is closed itself
server = CreateTCPServer(47124)
client = OpenTCPStream("127.0.0.1", 47124)
conn = AcceptTCPStream(server)
CloseTCPServer server
WriteLine client, "still open"
Print ReadLine(conn)        ; still open
CloseTCPStream client
CloseTCPStream conn
```

### AcceptTCPStream

`AcceptTCPStream(server%)` → integer

Returns a stream for the next client that has connected to server, or 0 if none is waiting.

```basic
; check for a new client (0 means nobody is waiting)
server = CreateTCPServer(5002)
If server <> 0
    client = AcceptTCPStream(server)
    If client <> 0
        Print "A player connected"
        CloseTCPStream client
    EndIf
    CloseTCPServer server
EndIf
```

### TCPStreamIP

`TCPStreamIP(stream%)` → integer

Returns the integer IP address of the other end of the TCP stream; use DottedIP to show it.

```basic
; show who connected
server = CreateTCPServer(5003)
If server <> 0
    client = AcceptTCPStream(server)
    If client <> 0 Then Print "From " + DottedIP(TCPStreamIP(client))
    CloseTCPServer server
EndIf
```

### TCPStreamPort

`TCPStreamPort(stream%)` → integer

Returns the port of the other end of the TCP stream.

```basic
; the port of the other end of a connection
server = CreateTCPServer(5004)
If server <> 0
    client = AcceptTCPStream(server)
    If client <> 0 Then Print "Remote port: " + TCPStreamPort(client)
    CloseTCPServer server
EndIf
```

### TCPTimeouts

`TCPTimeouts read_millis%, accept_millis%`

Sets how long TCP reads wait for data (read_millis) and how long AcceptTCPStream waits for a client (accept_millis), in milliseconds.

```basic
; wait up to 2 seconds for data, and 100 ms for a new client
TCPTimeouts 2000, 100
server = CreateTCPServer(5005)
If server <> 0
    client = AcceptTCPStream(server)   ; gives up after 100 ms
    CloseTCPServer server
EndIf
```

### CreateUDPStream

`CreateUDPStream([port%=0])` → integer

Opens a UDP stream on port (0 picks a free port) and returns its handle, or 0 on failure.

```basic
; open a UDP stream on any free port
udp = CreateUDPStream()
If udp <> 0
    Print "Using port " + UDPStreamPort(udp)
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

### CloseUDPStream

`CloseUDPStream stream%`

Closes the UDP stream and frees its port so another stream can use it. Its handle is invalid afterwards.

```basic
; free the port when done so another stream can use it
udp = CreateUDPStream(47126)
Print UDPStreamPort(udp)        ; 47126
CloseUDPStream udp
again = CreateUDPStream(47126)
Print again <> 0        ; 1
CloseUDPStream again
```

### SendUDPMsg

`SendUDPMsg stream%, dest_ip%, [dest_port%=0]`

Sends the data written to the UDP stream as one message to the integer IP address dest_ip and dest_port (0 uses the stream's own port).

```basic
; write data into the stream, then send it as one message
udp = CreateUDPStream()
WriteInt udp, 42
WriteString udp, "hello"
SendUDPMsg udp, 2130706433, 6001   ; to 127.0.0.1, port 6001
CloseUDPStream udp
```

### RecvUDPMsg

`RecvUDPMsg(stream%)` → integer

Receives the next UDP message on stream so it can be read with the stream commands, and returns the sender's integer IP address, or 0 if there is none.

```basic
; receive a message and read it with the stream commands
udp = CreateUDPStream(6002)
If udp <> 0
    sender = RecvUDPMsg(udp)
    If sender <> 0
        Print "Message from " + DottedIP(sender)
        Print ReadInt(udp)
    EndIf
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

### UDPStreamIP

`UDPStreamIP(stream%)` → integer

Returns the local integer IP address of the UDP stream.

```basic
; the local address of the UDP stream
udp = CreateUDPStream()
If udp <> 0
    Print "Local IP: " + DottedIP(UDPStreamIP(udp))
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

### UDPStreamPort

`UDPStreamPort(stream%)` → integer

Returns the port the UDP stream is using. Most useful after CreateUDPStream with port 0, which lets the system choose a free one. Gives an error if stream is not a UDP stream.

```basic
; the local port the stream is using
udp = CreateUDPStream(0)
If udp <> 0
    Print "Port: " + UDPStreamPort(udp)
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

### UDPMsgIP

`UDPMsgIP(stream%)` → integer

Returns the integer IP address of the sender of the last message received on the UDP stream.

```basic
; where the last message came from
udp = CreateUDPStream(6003)
If udp <> 0
    If RecvUDPMsg(udp) <> 0 Then Print "From " + DottedIP(UDPMsgIP(udp))
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

### UDPMsgPort

`UDPMsgPort(stream%)` → integer

Returns the port of the sender of the last message received on the UDP stream.

```basic
; the port the last message was sent from, so you can reply
udp = CreateUDPStream(6004)
If udp <> 0
    If RecvUDPMsg(udp) <> 0 Then Print "Reply to port " + UDPMsgPort(udp)
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

### UDPTimeouts

`UDPTimeouts recv_millis%`

Sets how long RecvUDPMsg waits for a message, in milliseconds (recv_millis).

```basic
; wait up to 50 ms for a message
UDPTimeouts 50
udp = CreateUDPStream(6005)
If udp <> 0
    If RecvUDPMsg(udp) = 0 Then Print "Nothing arrived"
    CloseUDPStream udp
EndIf
```

## Multiplayer games

### StartNetGame

`StartNetGame()` → integer

Joins a game found on the local network, or hosts a new one if there is none. Returns 1 if it joined, 2 if it is hosting, or 0 on failure. Only finds other copies of this program. The window stays alive while it searches.

```basic
; join a game on the network, or host one if none is found
result = StartNetGame()
If result = 1 Then Print "Joined a game"
If result = 2 Then Print "Hosting a new game"
If result = 0 Then Print "Could not start"
StopNetGame
```

### HostNetGame

`HostNetGame(game_name$)` → integer

Hosts a multiplayer game called game_name that others can join, and returns 2 on success or 0 on failure.

```basic
; host a game that others can join
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    Print "Hosting"
    StopNetGame
Else
    Print "Could not host"
EndIf
```

### JoinNetGame

`JoinNetGame(game_name$, server_ip$)` → integer

Joins the game game_name at server_ip, which can be an address, an invite from NetInvite, or empty to search the local network. Returns 1 on success or 0 on failure.

```basic
; join a game on the local network (empty address = search)
If JoinNetGame("Space Race", "") = 1
    Print "Joined"
    StopNetGame
Else
    Print "No game found"
EndIf
```

### StopNetGame

`StopNetGame`

Leaves the multiplayer game you joined, or shuts down the one you host. The players created here are gone, and CreateNetPlayer fails until you host or join again. Does nothing if no game is running.

```basic
; host a game, play, and leave when the round ends
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    me = CreateNetPlayer("Alice")
    Print "Playing as " + NetPlayerName(me)        ; Playing as Alice
    StopNetGame
    Print "Left the game"        ; Left the game
EndIf
```

### CreateNetPlayer

`CreateNetPlayer(name$)` → integer

Creates a local player called name in the current game and returns its player id.

```basic
; add the local player to the game
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    player = CreateNetPlayer("Alice")
    Print "My player id is " + player
    StopNetGame
EndIf
```

### DeleteNetPlayer

`DeleteNetPlayer player%`

Removes player from the game, so it disappears from the player list. Does nothing if there is no game. Players you created are also gone after StopNetGame.

```basic
; remove the local player from the game
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    player = CreateNetPlayer("Alice")
    DeleteNetPlayer player
    StopNetGame
EndIf
```

### NetPlayerName

`NetPlayerName(player%)` → string

Returns the name player was given when it was created with CreateNetPlayer, or an empty string if there is no such player or no game.

```basic
; show the name of a player
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    player = CreateNetPlayer("Alice")
    Print NetPlayerName(player)   ; Alice
    StopNetGame
EndIf
```

### NetPlayerLocal

`NetPlayerLocal(player%)` → integer

Returns 1 if player was created on this computer with CreateNetPlayer, or 0 if it belongs to another computer, does not exist, or no game is running. Use it to tell your own players from remote ones.

```basic
; true for players created on this computer
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    player = CreateNetPlayer("Alice")
    If NetPlayerLocal(player) Then Print "That is me"
    StopNetGame
EndIf
```

### SendNetMsg

`SendNetMsg(type%, data$, from_player%, [to_player%=0], [reliable%=1])` → integer

Sends a message of type (1-99) with the text data from from_player to to_player (0 sends to all players). Returns true on success. With reliable set to 0 the message is faster but may be lost or arrive out of order: use that for things sent again and again, such as positions.

```basic
; send a type-1 message from me to everyone (to_player = 0)
If HostNetGame("Space Race") = 2
    me = CreateNetPlayer("Alice")
    ok = SendNetMsg(1, "hello everyone", me, 0)
    If ok Then Print "Sent"
    StopNetGame
EndIf
```

### RecvNetMsg

`RecvNetMsg()` → integer

Fetches the next received message and returns true, or false if there is none; read it with NetMsgType, NetMsgFrom, NetMsgTo and NetMsgData.

```basic
; handle every message that has arrived
While RecvNetMsg()
    Print "Type " + NetMsgType() + " from player " + NetMsgFrom() + ": " + NetMsgData()
Wend
```

### NetMsgType

`NetMsgType()` → integer

Returns the type of the last received message: 1-99 for player messages, 100 for a player joining, 101 for a player leaving and 200 when the session is lost.

```basic
; tell player messages apart from joins and leaves
While RecvNetMsg()
    kind = NetMsgType()
    If kind = 100 Then Print "A player joined"
    If kind = 101 Then Print "A player left"
    If kind = 200 Then Print "The session was lost"
    If kind >= 1 And kind <= 99 Then Print "Player message"
Wend
```

### NetMsgFrom

`NetMsgFrom()` → integer

Returns the player id that sent the last received message (for type 100, the new player).

```basic
; find out who sent the message
While RecvNetMsg()
    sender = NetMsgFrom()
    Print NetPlayerName(sender) + " says " + NetMsgData()
Wend
```

### NetMsgTo

`NetMsgTo()` → integer

Returns the player id the last received message was sent to, or 0 if it was sent to everyone.

```basic
; 0 means the message was sent to everyone
While RecvNetMsg()
    If NetMsgTo() = 0 Then Print "Broadcast: " + NetMsgData()
Wend
```

### NetMsgData

`NetMsgData()` → string

Returns the text of the message RecvNetMsg last received, or an empty string before any. Read it right after RecvNetMsg returns 1, because the next message replaces it.

```basic
; read the text of the message
While RecvNetMsg()
    Print "Received: " + NetMsgData()
Wend
```

### NetPlay

`NetPlay(game$, [player_name$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** The easy way to start: joins a game called game on the local network, or hosts one if there is none, and creates your player. Returns 1 if it joined, 2 if it is hosting, or 0 on failure (see NetError). The window stays alive while it searches.

```basic
; find a game of this name on the network, or start one
If NetPlay("Coin Rush", "Alice") = 0 Then RuntimeError NetError()
Print "Playing as " + NetPlayerName(NetMe())
```

### NetCreate

`NetCreate(game$, [player_name$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** Hosts a new game called game and creates your player. Returns 2, or 0 on failure. Use it for a "Host" button; NetPlay decides by itself.

```basic
; always start a new game, for a "Host" button in a menu
If NetCreate("Coin Rush") = 2 Then Print "Invite: " + NetInvite()
```

### NetJoin

`NetJoin(invite$, [player_name$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** Joins a game with an invite from NetInvite, from anywhere on the internet, and creates your player. Returns 1, or 0 on failure (see NetError).

```basic
; join a friend's game with the code they sent you
If NetJoin(CommandLine(), "Bob") = 0 Then Print "Could not join: " + NetError()
```

### NetInvite

`NetInvite()` → string

**(ext)** Returns the code that lets others join this game with NetJoin, or an empty string if there is no game. Every player in the game can get it, and it lasts as long as the game.

```basic
; show the code that lets a friend join from anywhere
If NetCreate("Coin Rush") Then Print NetInvite()
```

### NetError

`NetError()` → string

**(ext)** Returns why the last game could not start or was lost, in words you can show the player.

```basic
; say why a game could not start
If NetPlay("Coin Rush") = 0 Then Print NetError()
```

### NetIsHost

`NetIsHost()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 if this computer is hosting the game. Let the host decide things like who won, so nobody disagrees.

```basic
; one computer decides who won
If NetIsHost() Then NetSet "round", 1
```

### NetMe

`NetMe()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the player id of this computer's main player, or 0 if there is none.

```basic
; your own player
If NetPlay("Coin Rush") Then Print "My name: " + NetPlayerName(NetMe())
```

### NetHostPlayer

`NetHostPlayer()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the player id of the host's main player, for sending it something with NetSend.

```basic
; send something to whoever is hosting
NetSend "ready", "", NetHostPlayer()
```

### NetCountPlayers

`NetCountPlayers()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many players are in the game.

```basic
; list everybody
For i = 1 To NetCountPlayers()
    Print NetPlayerName(NetPlayerAt(i))
Next
```

### NetPlayerAt

`NetPlayerAt(index%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the player id of the player at index, from 1 to NetCountPlayers, in the order they joined; 0 if there is no such player.

```basic
; the first player to join is number 1
oldest = NetPlayerAt(1)
Print NetPlayerName(oldest)
```

### NetPing

`NetPing()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the time for a message to reach the host and come back, in milliseconds. Always 0 on the host.

```basic
; show how long the network takes
Print "Ping: " + NetPing() + " ms"
```

### NetSet

`NetSet key$, value$`

**(ext)** Sets a value shared by every computer in the game, including anyone who joins later. Numbers can be used as value.

```basic
; every computer gets the same value, and so does anybody who joins later
NetSet "round", 3
```

### NetGet

`NetGet(key$, [default$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns a value set with NetSet, or default if nobody has set it. It is text; a number variable converts it for you.

```basic
; a shared value, or a default if nobody has set it
round = Int(NetGet("round", "1"))
Print "Round " + round
```

### NetSend

`NetSend(event$, [data$=""], [to_player%=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Sends an event called event with the text data to to_player, or everyone else if to_player is 0. Always arrives, in order. Returns true on success.

```basic
; tell everybody something happened, or pass a player id to tell one
NetSend "boom", "12,40"
```

### NetRecv

`NetRecv()` → integer

**(ext)** Fetches the next thing that happened and returns true, or false if there is nothing. Read it with NetEvent, NetData, NetFrom and NetEntity. Besides events from NetSend, it reports "join", "leave", "spawn", "despawn" and "lost" (the game ended).

```basic
; read what happened since the last frame
While NetRecv()
    Print NetEvent() + " from " + NetPlayerName(NetFrom())
Wend
```

### NetEvent

`NetEvent()` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of the event NetRecv last fetched.

```basic
; the kind of thing that happened
While NetRecv()
    If NetEvent() = "join" Then Print NetData() + " joined"
    If NetEvent() = "lost" Then Print "The game has ended"
Wend
```

### NetData

`NetData()` → string

**(ext)** Returns the text sent with the event NetRecv last fetched. For "join" it is the player's name; for "spawn" the template's name; for "lost" the reason.

```basic
; the text sent with an event
While NetRecv()
    If NetEvent() = "boom" Then Print "Explosion at " + NetData()
Wend
```

### NetFrom

`NetFrom()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the player id the event NetRecv last fetched came from. For "spawn" and "despawn", the entity's owner.

```basic
; who an event came from
While NetRecv()
    Print NetPlayerName(NetFrom())
Wend
```

### NetEntity

`NetEntity()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the entity a "spawn" or "despawn" event from NetRecv is about. After "despawn" it has already been freed, so only compare it.

```basic
; the entity a "spawn" event is about
While NetRecv()
    If NetEvent() = "spawn" Then EntityColor NetEntity(), 255, 80, 80
Wend
```

### NetSimulate

`NetSimulate lag_millis%, [loss_percent%=0]`

**(ext)** Makes the network pretend to be bad, for testing: delays what arrives by lag_millis and loses loss_percent of the messages that are allowed to be lost. NetSimulate 0, 0 stops it.

```basic
; test with 150 ms of lag and 10% of fast messages lost
NetSimulate 150, 10
```

### NetRelay

`NetRelay url$`

**(ext)** Uses your own relay server at url for games started after this, instead of the free public ones meant for development. Every player must use the same one.

```basic
; use your own relay server (set it before starting a game)
NetRelay "https://relay.example.com"
result = NetPlay("Coin Rush")
```

### NetTemplate

`NetTemplate name$, entity%`

**(ext)** Registers entity as what copies called name look like, and hides it. Every computer must register the same names. Keep physics off the template: copies on other computers only follow their owner. If the entity has a character controller, it becomes the pattern for a controller on every copy, moved by the host's rules (see NetCharacter), and its own handle stops working.

```basic
; what a "kart" looks like, on every computer
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
kart = CreateCube()
NetTemplate "kart", kart
```

### NetSpawn

`NetSpawn(template$, [x#=0], [y#=0], [z#=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Creates a copy of a template at x, y, z that belongs to this computer and returns it. Move it with the usual commands, and it moves smoothly on every other computer. FreeEntity removes it everywhere, and it goes when its owner leaves.

```basic
; a kart everyone can see; move it with the usual commands
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
If NetPlay("Karts") = 0 Then RuntimeError NetError()
NetTemplate "kart", CreateCube()
mine = NetSpawn("kart", 0, 1, 0)
MoveEntity mine, 0, 0, 1
```

### NetIsMine

`NetIsMine(entity%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 if entity was made with NetSpawn on this computer.

```basic
; move only what belongs to this computer
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
kart = CreateCube()
If NetIsMine(kart) Then MoveEntity kart, 0, 0, 1
```

### NetOwner

`NetOwner(entity%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the player id entity belongs to, or 0 if it is not a networked entity.

```basic
; which player an entity belongs to
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
kart = CreateCube()
Print NetPlayerName(NetOwner(kart))
```

### NetCharacter

`NetCharacter(entity%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the character controller of entity, if its template had one, or 0. Give it input with CharMove and UpdateChar as usual: the player's character answers at once and the host checks it. On the host, it also works on other players' characters, so CharImpulse and CharSet reach them.

```basic
; a character the host checks, used like any other
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
If NetPlay("Runners") = 0 Then RuntimeError NetError()
hero = CreateCube()
pattern = CreateCharController(hero, 0.4, 1.8)
CharSet pattern, "speed", 7
NetTemplate "hero", hero
player = NetCharacter(NetSpawn("hero", 0, 1, 0))
CharMove player, 1, 0, 0
UpdateChar player
```

### NetFire

`NetFire(event$, source_entity%, [range#=1000], [data$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** Tells the host a shot called event was fired along the forward direction of source_entity, up to range, along with when the shooter saw the world as it looks now. Returns true if it was sent. The host receives it from NetRecv and judges it with NetHit.

```basic
; fire along the camera, and let the host decide what that hit
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
If NetPlay("Duel") = 0 Then RuntimeError NetError()
camera = CreateCamera()
If MouseHit(1) Then NetFire "shot", camera, 100, "pistol"
```

### NetHit

`NetHit([radius#=0])` → integer

**(ext)** On the host, right after NetRecv fetched a NetFire: puts everything networked back where the shooter saw it, picks along the shot like LinePick (radius 0 is a thin line), and returns the entity hit or 0. Afterwards PickedX and the other Picked commands describe the hit. It never hits the shooter's own entities, and looks back at most half a second. Returns 0 on other computers and for other events. Targets need EntityPickMode, as for any pick.

```basic
; the host judges each shot against what the shooter could see
While NetRecv()
    If NetEvent() = "shot"
        target = NetHit()
        If target <> 0 Then NetSend "hit", NetData() + " at " + PickedX(), NetOwner(target)
    EndIf
Wend
```

### NetCorrections

`NetCorrections(character%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many times the host has disagreed with where this computer thought character was. Always 0 on the host.

```basic
; how often the host has had to put this character right
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2
If NetPlay("Runners") = 0 Then RuntimeError NetError()
hero = CreateCube()
pattern = CreateCharController(hero, 0.4, 1.8)
NetTemplate "hero", hero
player = NetCharacter(NetSpawn("hero"))
Print NetCorrections(player)
```

### NetRate

`NetRate updates_per_second#`

**(ext)** Sets how many times a second entity positions are sent, from 1 to 60. The default is 20.

```basic
; send positions 30 times a second, for a fast game
NetRate 30
```

### DottedIP

`DottedIP(ip%)` → string

Returns the integer IP address ip as a dotted string such as "192.168.0.1".

```basic
; show the address of a player as text: 192.168.0.1 is the number 3232235521
Print DottedIP(3232235521)        ; 192.168.0.1
```

### CountHostIPs

`CountHostIPs(host_name$)` → integer

Looks up the IP addresses of host_name (an empty string for this computer) and returns how many were found.

```basic
; look up this computer's addresses
count = CountHostIPs("")
Print count + " address(es) found"
For index = 1 To count
    Print DottedIP(HostIP(index))
Next
```

### HostIP

`HostIP(index%)` → integer

Returns address number index (from 1) found by the last CountHostIPs, as an integer IP address.

```basic
; show the first address of a host
If CountHostIPs("localhost") > 0
    Print DottedIP(HostIP(1))
EndIf
```

## Plugins and libraries

### CallDLL

`CallDLL(dll_name$, proc_name$, [in_bank%=0], [out_bank%=0])` → integer

Calls the function proc_name in the shared library dll_name, passing the contents of in_bank and out_bank (0 for none); the function can write results into out_bank. Returns the function's integer result. The library stays loaded.

```basic
; call a function in a shared library, passing it a bank
input = CreateBank(8)
PokeInt input, 0, 21
output = CreateBank(8)
result = CallDLL("mylib", "Double", input, output)   ; the function can write into output
Print "Function returned " + result
Print "Output: " + PeekInt(output, 0)
```

## Graphics and display

### Graphics3D

`Graphics3D width%, height%, [depth%=0], [mode%=0]`

Opens a window of width by height pixels for 3D and 2D graphics. mode 0 or 2 gives a window, 1 fullscreen, 3 a scaled window; depth is accepted for compatibility.

```basic
; Open a 800 by 600 window that can draw 3D and 2D
Graphics3D 800, 600, 32, 2   ; mode 2 = window, 1 = fullscreen, 3 = scaled window
camera = CreateCamera()
RenderWorld
Flip
```

### Graphics

`Graphics width%, height%, [depth%=0], [mode%=0]`

Opens a window of width by height pixels for 2D graphics; mode 0 or 2 gives a window, 1 fullscreen, 3 a scaled window.

```basic
; Open a 640 by 480 window for 2D drawing only
Graphics 640, 480, 32, 2   ; mode 2 = window
Text 10, 10, "Hello"
Flip
```

### EndGraphics

`EndGraphics`

Accepted for compatibility; does nothing. The window stays open and drawing keeps working, so there is no need to call it when your program ends.

```basic
; Close down graphics when the program is finished
Graphics 640, 480
Text 10, 10, "Bye"
Flip
EndGraphics
```

### AppTitle

`AppTitle title$, [close_prompt$=""]`

Sets the window title to title. close_prompt is accepted for compatibility.

```basic
; put the game's name and version in the window title (can be set before Graphics)
version$ = "0.3"
AppTitle "Asteroid Miner v" + version$
Graphics 640, 480
Text 10, 10, "Look at the window title bar"
Flip
WaitKey
```

### SetFullscreen

`SetFullscreen enable%`

**(ext)** Switches between a window (enable = 0) and borderless fullscreen (enable = 1) at any time.

```basic
; Switch between a window and fullscreen while the program runs
Graphics 640, 480
SetFullscreen 1   ; 1 = borderless fullscreen
SetFullscreen 0   ; 0 = back to a window
```

### IsFullscreen

`IsFullscreen()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 if the program is running fullscreen and 0 if it is in a window. It follows the mode given to Graphics or Graphics3D and any later SetFullscreen call.

```basic
; Find out if the program is running fullscreen
Graphics 640, 480
If IsFullscreen() Then Print "Fullscreen" Else Print "In a window"
```

### GfxMode3D

`GfxMode3D(mode%)` → integer

Returns true if graphics mode number mode exists and supports 3D.

```basic
; pick the first listed screen size of at least 1024 wide that supports 3D
For mode = 1 To CountGfxModes()
    If GfxMode3D(mode) And GfxModeWidth(mode) >= 1024 Then Exit
Next
Print "Using mode " + mode + ": " + GfxModeWidth(mode) + " x " + GfxModeHeight(mode)   ; Using mode 3: 1024 x 768
; then open it with: Graphics3D GfxModeWidth(mode), GfxModeHeight(mode)
```

### GfxDriver3D

`GfxDriver3D(driver%)` → integer

Returns true if graphics driver number driver supports 3D (only driver 1 exists).

```basic
; list every graphics driver that can do 3D
For driver = 1 To CountGfxDrivers()
    If GfxDriver3D(driver) Then Print driver + ": " + GfxDriverName(driver)   ; 1: OpenGL 3.3 (Fyrox)
Next
Print "Driver 2 supports 3D: " + GfxDriver3D(2)   ; 0, there is no driver 2
```

### WBuffer

`WBuffer enable%`

Accepted for compatibility; depth precision is handled by the renderer.

```basic
; a typical Blitz3D start-up block; WBuffer is accepted but changes nothing
WBuffer True
HWMultiTex True
Graphics3D 800, 600
cam = CreateCamera()
cube = CreateCube()
MoveEntity cube, 0, 0, 5
RenderWorld
Flip
WaitKey
```

### ActiveTextures

`ActiveTextures()` → integer

Returns the number of different textures currently applied to entities.

```basic
; Count the textures that are in use by entities
Graphics3D 800, 600
cube = CreateCube()
tex = LoadTexture("checker.png")
EntityTexture cube, tex
Print "Textures in use: " + ActiveTextures()
```

### GraphicsDepth

`GraphicsDepth()` → integer

Returns the colour depth of the display in bits (always 32).

```basic
; Print the colour depth of the display in bits
Graphics 640, 480
Print "Colour depth: " + GraphicsDepth()
```

### CountGfxModes

`CountGfxModes()` → integer

Returns how many screen sizes are listed: 17 common sizes from 640 x 480 up to 3840 x 2160, not a hardware scan. Use mode numbers 1 to this count with GfxModeWidth and GfxModeHeight.

```basic
; Print every listed screen size
For mode = 1 To CountGfxModes()
    Print GfxModeWidth(mode) + " x " + GfxModeHeight(mode)
Next
```

### CountGfxModes3D

`CountGfxModes3D()` → integer

Returns how many screen sizes are listed for 3D. It is the same 17 as CountGfxModes because every listed size works in 3D. Mode numbers run from 1 to this count.

```basic
; show every screen size you could open a 3D window at
For mode = 1 To CountGfxModes3D()
    Print mode + ": " + GfxModeWidth(mode) + " x " + GfxModeHeight(mode)
Next
Print "Total: " + CountGfxModes3D()   ; Total: 17
```

### GfxModeWidth

`GfxModeWidth(mode%)` → integer

Returns the width in pixels of graphics mode number mode, or 0 if it does not exist.

```basic
; find the widest listed screen size
widest = 0
For mode = 1 To CountGfxModes()
    If GfxModeWidth(mode) > widest Then widest = GfxModeWidth(mode)
Next
Print "Widest: " + widest   ; Widest: 3840
Print "Mode 99 width: " + GfxModeWidth(99)   ; 0, there is no mode 99
```

### GfxModeHeight

`GfxModeHeight(mode%)` → integer

Returns the height in pixels of graphics mode number mode, or 0 if it does not exist.

```basic
; find the height that goes with a 1280 wide mode
For mode = 1 To CountGfxModes()
    If GfxModeWidth(mode) = 1280 Then Exit
Next
Print "A 1280 wide mode is " + GfxModeHeight(mode) + " tall"   ; 720
Print "Mode 99 height: " + GfxModeHeight(99)   ; 0, there is no mode 99
```

### GfxModeDepth

`GfxModeDepth(mode%)` → integer

Returns the colour depth of graphics mode number mode (32), or 0 if it does not exist.

```basic
; check the colour depth of each listed mode (0 means the mode does not exist)
Print "Mode 1 depth: " + GfxModeDepth(1)   ; 32
Print "Mode 99 depth: " + GfxModeDepth(99)   ; 0
If GfxModeDepth(1) = 32 Then Print "True colour is available"
```

### GfxModeExists

`GfxModeExists(width%, height%, [depth%=0])` → integer

Returns true if width by height is one of the listed graphics modes; any size works in a window.

```basic
; choose a screen size, falling back to a standard one if it is not listed
width = 1000 : height = 700
If Not GfxModeExists(width, height) Then
    width = 1024 : height = 768
EndIf
Print "Opening at " + width + " x " + height   ; Opening at 1024 x 768
; Graphics3D width, height
```

### GfxMode3DExists

`GfxMode3DExists(width%, height%, [depth%=0])` → integer

Returns true if width by height is one of the listed 3D graphics modes; any size works in a window.

```basic
; choose a 3D screen size, falling back to a standard one if it is not listed
width = 1280 : height = 720
If Not GfxMode3DExists(width, height) Then
    width = 800 : height = 600
EndIf
Print "Opening at " + width + " x " + height   ; Opening at 1280 x 720
; Graphics3D width, height
```

### CountGfxDrivers

`CountGfxDrivers()` → integer

Returns how many graphics drivers there are. This is always 1, the renderer NeptuneBASIC uses, so driver number 1 is the only valid one.

```basic
; offer a driver menu only if there is a choice (there never is here)
drivers = CountGfxDrivers()
If drivers > 1 Then
    For d = 1 To drivers
        Print d + ": " + GfxDriverName(d)
    Next
Else
    Print "Only one graphics driver, using it"   ; always printed
EndIf
```

### GfxDriverName

`GfxDriverName(driver%)` → string

Returns the name of graphics driver number driver, or an empty string if it does not exist.

```basic
; show the driver's name, for a bug report or an "about" screen
Print "Driver 1: " + GfxDriverName(1)   ; Driver 1: OpenGL 3.3 (Fyrox)
If GfxDriverName(2) = "" Then Print "There is no driver 2"
```

### SetGfxDriver

`SetGfxDriver driver%`

Accepted for compatibility; does nothing, because there is only one graphics driver (number 1). Old programs can still call it from a driver menu without harm.

```basic
; Blitz3D programs chose a driver before opening the window; this keeps them working
SetGfxDriver CountGfxDrivers()
Graphics3D 800, 600
Text 10, 10, "Window open with the only driver"
Flip
WaitKey
```

### Windowed3D

`Windowed3D()` → integer

Returns true if 3D graphics can run in a window (always true).

```basic
; ask for a window only if the driver can do 3D in one (it always can)
If Windowed3D() Then mode = 2 Else mode = 1   ; 2 = windowed, 1 = fullscreen
Graphics3D 800, 600, 0, mode
Text 10, 10, "Mode " + mode
Flip
WaitKey
```

### GfxDriverCaps3D

`GfxDriverCaps3D()` → integer

Returns the 3D capability level of the graphics driver (always 110).

```basic
; old programs tested the driver's level before using fancy effects
level = GfxDriverCaps3D()
Print "Capability level: " + level   ; 110
If level >= 110 Then Print "Full feature set available"
```

### HWTexUnits

`HWTexUnits()` → integer

Returns the number of texture layers an entity can use at once.

```basic
; see whether an entity can blend a base texture and a light map
layers = HWTexUnits()
Print "Texture layers per entity: " + layers   ; 4
If layers >= 2 Then Print "Multi-texturing is available"
```

### HWMultiTex

`HWMultiTex enable%`

Accepted for compatibility; does nothing. Entities can always blend several texture layers (see HWTexUnits), so there is no multi-texturing switch to turn on.

```basic
; old programs switched multi-texturing on or off to suit the card; it is always available
HWMultiTex False
Graphics3D 800, 600
Print "Texture layers: " + HWTexUnits()
Flip
WaitKey
```

### Dither

`Dither enable%`

Accepted for compatibility; does nothing. Colour dithering is not used, so you cannot turn it on or off.

```basic
; old programs turned dithering on for 16-bit screens; it has no effect here
Dither True
Graphics 640, 480
Text 10, 10, "Dither does nothing, nothing to see"
Flip
WaitKey
```

### TotalVidMem

`TotalVidMem()` → integer

Returns the total video memory in bytes; a fixed figure of 1 GB, since it cannot be read.

```basic
; Print the total video memory in megabytes
megabytes = TotalVidMem() / 1048576
Print "Video memory: " + megabytes + " MB"
```

### AvailVidMem

`AvailVidMem()` → integer

Returns the free video memory in bytes; a fixed figure of 1 GB, since it cannot be read.

```basic
; Print the free video memory in megabytes
megabytes = AvailVidMem() / 1048576
Print "Free video memory: " + megabytes + " MB"
```

### VWait

`VWait [frames%=1]`

Accepted for compatibility; does nothing. Flip already waits for the display refresh, so a game loop that ends in Flip never needs it.

```basic
; old programs waited for the screen refresh before drawing; Flip already does that
Graphics 640, 480
While Not KeyHit(1)
    VWait 1
    Cls
    Text 10, 10, "Press Esc to quit"
    Flip
Wend
```

### ScanLine

`ScanLine()` → integer

Returns the display scan line, which is always 0. Never loop until the scan line reaches a value, because it never will; use Flip to keep in step with the display.

```basic
; old code polled the scan line to time its drawing; here it is always 0, so use Flip
line = ScanLine()
Print "Scan line: " + line   ; Scan line: 0
Graphics 640, 480
Cls
Text 10, 10, "Draw, then Flip to sync"
Flip
WaitKey
```

### TrisRendered

`TrisRendered()` → integer

Returns the number of triangles drawn by the last RenderWorld.

```basic
; Show how many triangles the last RenderWorld drew
Graphics3D 800, 600
camera = CreateCamera()
cube = CreateCube()
MoveEntity cube, 0, 0, 5
RenderWorld
Text 10, 10, "Triangles: " + TrisRendered()
Flip
```

### GraphicsWidth

`GraphicsWidth()` → integer

Returns the width in pixels of the drawing area set by Graphics or Graphics3D. Use it instead of a fixed number to centre things or lay out a screen at any size.

```basic
; Draw a line across the whole width of the window
Graphics 640, 480
Line 0, 240, GraphicsWidth() - 1, 240
Flip
```

### GraphicsHeight

`GraphicsHeight()` → integer

Returns the height in pixels of the drawing area set by Graphics or Graphics3D. Use it instead of a fixed number to place things at the bottom or middle of the screen.

```basic
; Put some text halfway down the window
Graphics 640, 480
middle = GraphicsHeight() / 2
Text 10, middle, "Halfway down", 0, 1   ; last 1 = centred vertically on that point
Flip
```

### UpdateWorld

`UpdateWorld [elapsed_time#=1]`

Updates animations, collisions and physics for one frame; elapsed_time scales how far animations advance (default 1).

```basic
; The main loop of a 3D game
Graphics3D 800, 600
camera = CreateCamera()
cube = CreateCube()
MoveEntity cube, 0, 0, 5
While Not KeyHit(1)   ; 1 = Escape
    TurnEntity cube, 0, 1, 0
    UpdateWorld   ; moves animations, collisions and physics on by one frame
    RenderWorld
    Flip
Wend
```

### RenderWorld

`RenderWorld [tween#=1]`

Renders the 3D world from every active camera, covering any 2D drawn before it. tween (0 to 1) blends each entity between its CaptureWorld position and its current one, for smooth fixed-rate loops; 1 draws the current positions.

```basic
; Draw the 3D world, then 2D text on top
Graphics3D 800, 600
camera = CreateCamera()
cube = CreateCube()
MoveEntity cube, 0, 0, 5
RenderWorld   ; tween 1 (the default) draws the current positions
Text 10, 10, "Score: 0"
Flip
```

### Flip

`Flip [vwait%=1]`

Shows the finished frame on screen and ends the frame; vwait (default 1) waits for the vertical blank.

```basic
; Show the finished frame on screen
Graphics 640, 480
While Not KeyHit(1)
    Cls
    Text 10, 10, "Press Escape to quit"
    Flip   ; waits for the display, so the loop runs at screen speed
Wend
```

### WireFrame

`WireFrame enable%`

Draws all 3D geometry as wireframe when enable is 1, solid when 0.

```basic
; Toggle wireframe drawing with the space bar
Graphics3D 800, 600
camera = CreateCamera()
cube = CreateCube()
MoveEntity cube, 0, 0, 5
showWires = 0
While Not KeyHit(1)
    If KeyHit(57) Then showWires = 1 - showWires   ; 57 = space
    WireFrame showWires   ; 1 = wireframe, 0 = solid
    TurnEntity cube, 0, 1, 0
    RenderWorld
    Flip
Wend
```

### ScreenFade

`ScreenFade red#, green#, blue#, alpha#, [seconds#=0.5]`

**(ext)** Fades a colour red, green, blue over the whole screen (3D and 2D) from its current opacity to alpha (0 clear, 1 solid) over seconds (default 0.5): fade to black between levels, flash white when hit. The colour stays at alpha until the next ScreenFade.

```basic
; fade to black, load the next level, fade back in
ScreenFade 0, 0, 0, 1, 0.5
While ScreenFading()
    RenderWorld
    Flip
Wend
ScreenFade 0, 0, 0, 0, 0.5
```

### ScreenFading

`ScreenFading()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 while a ScreenFade is still running and 0 once it has finished or if none was started. Use it to wait for a fade to end before changing level.

```basic
ScreenFade 255, 255, 255, 0, 0.3   ; a white flash fading out
If ScreenFading() Then Print "fading"
```

## Gamma and quality

### GraphicsQuality

`GraphicsQuality level%`

**(ext)** Sets the renderer quality preset: level 0 low, 1 medium, 2 high, 3 ultra (QUALITY_LOW to QUALITY_ULTRA). Use a low level for old graphics cards.

```basic
; Use a low quality preset for old graphics cards
Graphics3D 800, 600
GraphicsQuality QUALITY_LOW   ; 0 low, 1 medium, 2 high, 3 ultra
```

### GetGraphicsQuality

`GetGraphicsQuality()` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the quality level in use, 0 (low) to 3 (ultra). Until you set one it is 2 (high), unless the NEPTUNE_QUALITY environment variable chose another. It can be lower than the level GraphicsQuality asked for: the player's ceiling (neptune run --max-quality, or NEPTUNE_MAX_QUALITY) holds it down, and AutoQuality lowers it while the game runs slowly.

```basic
; Read the current quality level
Graphics3D 800, 600
GraphicsQuality 2
```
