```basic
Print "Quality level: " + GetGraphicsQuality()   ; 2 = high
```

### AutoQuality

`AutoQuality [fps#=50]`

**(ext)** Lets the quality level look after itself: while the game runs slower than fps frames a second, the level steps down one at a time. A step that does not make the game faster is taken back, and the level is then left alone (the graphics card was not what held the game back). The level never rises by itself. AutoQuality 0 turns this off. Players can turn it on for any program with neptune run --quality auto.

```basic
; start with everything turned up and let slow computers turn it down
Graphics3D 800, 600
GraphicsQuality QUALITY_ULTRA
AutoQuality 50                   ; aim for 50 frames a second or more
cam = CreateCamera()
While Not KeyHit(1)
    RenderWorld
    Text 10, 10, "Quality level: " + GetGraphicsQuality()
    Flip
Wend
```

### GraphicsCardName

`GraphicsCardName()` → string

**(ext)** Returns what the graphics card that draws the game calls itself, such as "Intel(R) HD Graphics 4400", for settings screens and bug reports. Empty before Graphics3D.

```basic
; Show which graphics card is drawing the game
Graphics3D 800, 600
Print "Graphics card: " + GraphicsCardName()
```

### PostEffect

`PostEffect effect%, [enable%=1]`

**(ext)** Turns a rendering effect on (enable = 1) or off (enable = 0): EFFECT_BLOOM, EFFECT_SSAO, EFFECT_FXAA, EFFECT_SHADOWS or EFFECT_LIGHT_SCATTER.

```basic
; Turn on bloom, and turn off shadows
Graphics3D 800, 600
PostEffect EFFECT_BLOOM, 1
PostEffect EFFECT_SHADOWS, 0
```

### AntiAlias

`AntiAlias enable%`

Turns anti-aliasing (smoothing of jagged edges) on (1) or off (0) using a fast screen filter (FXAA). It overrides the level's choice, but a later GraphicsQuality call resets it.

```basic
; Smooth out jagged edges
Graphics3D 800, 600
AntiAlias 1   ; 1 = on, 0 = off
```

### SetGamma

`SetGamma src_red%, src_green%, src_blue%, dest_red#, dest_green#, dest_blue#`

Sets the gamma ramp so that the colour levels src_red, src_green, src_blue (0-255) are shown as dest_red, dest_green, dest_blue. Call UpdateGamma to apply it.

```basic
; Make green brighter, then apply the change
Graphics 640, 480
SetGamma 128, 128, 128, 128, 160, 128   ; level 128 of each colour is shown as red 128, green 160, blue 128
UpdateGamma
```

### UpdateGamma

`UpdateGamma [calibrate%=0]`

Applies the gamma changes made with SetGamma; calibrate is accepted for compatibility.

```basic
; Apply changes made with SetGamma
Graphics 640, 480
SetGamma 255, 255, 255, 255, 255, 255
UpdateGamma
```

### GammaRed

`GammaRed(red%)` → float

Returns the output level the red level red (0-255) is mapped to by the gamma ramp.

```basic
; Read how the red level 128 is shown
Graphics 640, 480
Print "Red 128 is shown as " + GammaRed(128)
```

### GammaGreen

`GammaGreen(green%)` → float

Returns the output level the green level green (0-255) is mapped to by the gamma ramp.

```basic
; Read how the green level 128 is shown
Graphics 640, 480
Print "Green 128 is shown as " + GammaGreen(128)
```

### GammaBlue

`GammaBlue(blue%)` → float

Returns the output level the blue level blue (0-255) is mapped to by the gamma ramp.

```basic
; Read how the blue level 128 is shown
Graphics 640, 480
Print "Blue 128 is shown as " + GammaBlue(128)
```

## 2D drawing and text

### Locate

`Locate x%, y%`

Moves the text cursor used by Print and Write to x, y in pixels.

```basic
; Move the text cursor, then print there
Graphics 640, 480
Locate 100, 50   ; x, y in pixels
Print "Printed at 100, 50"
Flip
```

### Viewport

`Viewport x%, y%, width%, height%`

Restricts 2D drawing to the rectangle at x, y with size width by height in pixels.

```basic
; Only draw inside a 200 by 100 box
Graphics 640, 480
Viewport 50, 50, 200, 100
Color 255, 0, 0
Rect 0, 0, 640, 480   ; only the part inside the box is drawn
Viewport 0, 0, 640, 480   ; back to the whole window
Flip
```

### Text

`Text x%, y%, string$, [center_x%=0], [center_y%=0]`

Draws string at x, y in the current font and colour; center_x and center_y set to 1 centre it horizontally or vertically on that point.

```basic
; Draw left aligned text, and text centred on a point
Graphics 640, 480
Text 10, 10, "Top left"
Text 320, 240, "Centre", 1, 1   ; centred across and down
Flip
```

### Color

`Color red%, green%, blue%`

Sets the drawing colour for 2D commands, with red, green, blue from 0 to 255.

```basic
; Draw a red rectangle
Graphics 640, 480
Color 255, 0, 0   ; red, green, blue from 0 to 255
Rect 100, 100, 200, 50
Flip
```

### ColorRed

`ColorRed()` → integer

Returns the red part (0-255) of the current drawing colour.

```basic
; Read the red part of the drawing colour
Graphics 640, 480
Color 200, 100, 50
Print "Red: " + ColorRed()
```

### ColorGreen

`ColorGreen()` → integer

Returns the green part (0-255) of the current drawing colour.

```basic
; Read the green part of the drawing colour
Graphics 640, 480
Color 200, 100, 50
Print "Green: " + ColorGreen()
```

### ColorBlue

`ColorBlue()` → integer

Returns the blue part (0-255) of the current drawing colour.

```basic
; Read the blue part of the drawing colour
Graphics 640, 480
Color 200, 100, 50
Print "Blue: " + ColorBlue()
```

### ClsColor

`ClsColor red%, green%, blue%`

Sets the colour Cls clears to, with red, green, blue from 0 to 255.

```basic
; Clear the screen to dark blue every frame
Graphics 640, 480
ClsColor 0, 0, 80
Cls
Flip
```

### Cls

`Cls`

Clears the current buffer to the ClsColor colour and puts the Print and Text cursor back at the top left. Call it once at the start of every frame; in a 3D scene it clears only the 2D drawing, not the 3D world.

```basic
; Clear the screen, then draw a fresh frame
Graphics 640, 480
Cls
Text 10, 10, "A fresh screen"
Flip
```

### Origin

`Origin x%, y%`

Sets the point x, y (pixels) that 2D drawing commands are measured from.

```basic
; Draw relative to the middle of the screen
Graphics 640, 480
Origin 320, 240
Oval -20, -20, 40, 40   ; centred on the screen
Flip
```

### Rect

`Rect x%, y%, width%, height%, [solid%=1]`

Draws a rectangle at x, y of width by height pixels, filled when solid is 1 (default) or as an outline when 0.

```basic
; A filled box and an outlined box
Graphics 640, 480
Rect 50, 50, 100, 60   ; filled
Rect 200, 50, 100, 60, 0   ; 0 = outline only
Flip
```

### Oval

`Oval x%, y%, width%, height%, [solid%=1]`

Draws an ellipse inside the box x, y, width, height (pixels), filled when solid is 1 (default) or as an outline when 0.

```basic
; A filled circle and an outlined ellipse
Graphics 640, 480
Oval 50, 50, 80, 80   ; filled
Oval 200, 50, 120, 60, 0   ; 0 = outline only
Flip
```

### Line

`Line x1%, y1%, x2%, y2%`

Draws a line from x1, y1 to x2, y2 in pixels.

```basic
; Draw a diagonal line across the window
Graphics 640, 480
Line 0, 0, 639, 479   ; from the top left to the bottom right
Flip
```

### Plot

`Plot x%, y%`

Draws a single pixel at x, y in the current Color. x and y count from the top-left corner of the screen or buffer in pixels. Drawing outside the screen or buffer is harmless.

```basic
; Draw a single white pixel in the middle
Graphics 640, 480
Color 255, 255, 255
Plot 320, 240
Flip
```

## Fonts

### LoadFont

`LoadFont(fontname$, [height%=12], [bold%=0], [italic%=0], [underline%=0])` → integer

Loads a font fontname at height pixels, optionally bold, italic or underlined (1 = on). Returns the font handle, or 0 if it cannot be loaded.

```basic
; Load a large bold font and use it
Graphics 640, 480
bigFont = LoadFont("Arial", 32, 1)   ; name, height, bold
If bigFont <> 0 Then SetFont bigFont
Text 10, 10, "Big title"
Flip
```

### SetFont

`SetFont font%`

Makes font the font for Text and Print. Font 0 selects the built-in font (ext).

```basic
; Switch fonts, then go back to the built-in one
Graphics 640, 480
titleFont = LoadFont("Arial", 40)
SetFont titleFont
Text 10, 10, "Title"
SetFont 0   ; 0 = the built-in font
Text 10, 80, "Normal text"
Flip
```

### FreeFont

`FreeFont font%`

Frees font loaded with LoadFont. The handle is invalid afterwards, so SetFont with it is an error. Text keeps drawing with a font already chosen until you call SetFont for another.

```basic
; Free a font when it is no longer needed
Graphics 640, 480
titleFont = LoadFont("Arial", 40)
SetFont 0
FreeFont titleFont
```

### FontWidth

`FontWidth()` → integer

Returns the width of the widest character in the current font, in pixels.

```basic
; Print the width of the widest character
Graphics 640, 480
Print "Widest character: " + FontWidth() + " pixels"
```

### FontHeight

`FontHeight()` → integer

Returns the height in pixels of the current font, the built-in one or the last chosen with SetFont. Use it as the distance between lines of text.

```basic
; Space lines of text by the font height
Graphics 640, 480
lineHeight = FontHeight()
For row = 0 To 4
    Text 10, 10 + row * lineHeight, "Line " + row
Next
Flip
```

### StringWidth

`StringWidth(string$)` → integer

Returns the width of string in pixels in the current font.

```basic
; Centre text by measuring it
Graphics 640, 480
message = "Game Over"
Text (GraphicsWidth() - StringWidth(message)) / 2, 200, message
Flip
```

### StringHeight

`StringHeight(string$)` → integer

Returns the height of string in pixels in the current font.

```basic
; Draw a box around some text
Graphics 640, 480
message = "Press Enter"
Rect 8, 8, StringWidth(message) + 4, StringHeight(message) + 4, 0
Text 10, 10, message
Flip
```

## 2D images

### SaveImage

`SaveImage(image%, file$, [frame%=0])` → integer

Saves frame of image to an image file; returns 1 on success, 0 on failure.

```basic
; Make an image, draw on it, and save it to a file
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(64, 64)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(picture)
Color 255, 0, 0
Rect 0, 0, 64, 64
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
If SaveImage(picture, "red.png") Then Print "Saved" Else Print "Could not save"
```

### LoadImage

`LoadImage(file$)` → integer

Loads an image file for 2D drawing. Returns the image handle, or 0 on failure.

```basic
; Load a picture and draw it
Graphics 640, 480
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
If coin = 0 Then RuntimeError "coin.png not found"
DrawImage coin, 100, 100
Flip
```

### LoadAnimImage

`LoadAnimImage(file$, width%, height%, first%, count%)` → integer

Loads an image strip of frames, each width by height pixels, starting at frame first and loading count frames. Returns the image handle, or 0 on failure.

```basic
; Load a strip of 4 frames, each 32 by 32 pixels
Graphics 640, 480
explosion = LoadAnimImage("explosion.png", 32, 32, 0, 4)   ; width, height, first frame, number of frames
If explosion <> 0 Then DrawImage explosion, 100, 100, 2   ; draw frame 2
Flip
```

### FreeImage

`FreeImage image%`

Frees image and its memory. The handle is invalid afterwards: using it again, even in FreeImage, is a runtime error. Do not free an image you still draw.

```basic
; Free an image when it is no longer needed
Graphics 640, 480
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
FreeImage coin
```

### DrawImageEx

`DrawImageEx image%, x#, y#, [frame%=0], [rotation#=0], [scale_x#=1], [scale_y#=1], [alpha#=1]`

**(ext)** Draws frame of image at x,y rotated by rotation degrees clockwise and scaled by scale_x and scale_y about its handle, with alpha from 0 (invisible) to 1 (solid).

```basic
; Draw a ship turned 45 degrees, half size and half see-through
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
MidHandle ship
DrawImageEx ship, 320, 240, 0, 45, 0.5, 0.5, 0.5   ; frame, rotation, scale x, scale y, alpha
Flip
```

### DrawImage

`DrawImage image%, x%, y%, [frame%=0]`

Draws frame of image at x,y in pixels, with its mask colour transparent.

```basic
; Draw an image at the mouse position
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
MidHandle ship
While Not KeyHit(1)
    Cls
    DrawImage ship, MouseX(), MouseY()
    Flip
Wend
```

### DrawBlock

`DrawBlock image%, x%, y%, [frame%=0]`

Draws frame of image at x,y in pixels without transparency.

```basic
; Draw an image without transparency
Graphics 640, 480
tree = LoadImage("tree.png")
DrawBlock tree, 100, 100   ; the transparent parts are drawn solid
Flip
```

### DrawImageRect

`DrawImageRect image%, x%, y%, rect_x%, rect_y%, rect_width%, rect_height%, [frame%=0]`

Draws the part of image starting at rect_x,rect_y and rect_width by rect_height pixels at x,y, with its mask colour transparent.

```basic
; Draw only the top left 16 by 16 pixels of an image
Graphics 640, 480
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
DrawImageRect coin, 100, 100, 0, 0, 16, 16   ; x, y, then the part: left, top, width, height
Flip
```

### DrawBlockRect

`DrawBlockRect image%, x%, y%, rect_x%, rect_y%, rect_width%, rect_height%, [frame%=0]`

Draws the part of image starting at rect_x,rect_y and rect_width by rect_height pixels at x,y, without transparency.

```basic
; Draw only part of an image, without transparency
Graphics 640, 480
tree = LoadImage("tree.png")
DrawBlockRect tree, 100, 100, 0, 0, 32, 32
Flip
```

### CreateImage

`CreateImage(width%, height%, [frames%=1])` → integer

Creates a blank image width by height pixels with frames frames. Returns the image handle.

```basic
; Make a blank 3 frame image and fill the first frame
Graphics 640, 480
sprite = CreateImage(32, 32, 3)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(sprite, 0)
Color 0, 255, 0
Oval 0, 0, 32, 32
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
DrawImage sprite, 100, 100, 0
Flip
```

### CopyImage

`CopyImage(image%)` → integer

Returns a new image that is a copy of image.

```basic
; Make a copy, then resize the copy without changing the original
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
smallShip = CopyImage(ship)
ScaleImage smallShip, 0.5, 0.5
DrawImage ship, 100, 100
DrawImage smallShip, 300, 100
Flip
```

### GrabImage

`GrabImage image%, x%, y%, [frame%=0]`

Copies the area of the current buffer at x,y (minus the image handle) into frame of image.

```basic
; Take a picture of part of the screen
Graphics 640, 480
Color 255, 255, 0
Oval 0, 0, 50, 50
snapshot = CreateImage(50, 50)
GrabImage snapshot, 0, 0   ; copy the 50 by 50 area at 0, 0
Cls
DrawImage snapshot, 200, 200
Flip
```

### RotateImage

`RotateImage image%, angle#`

Turns the pixels of image by angle degrees clockwise, growing the picture to fit and keeping the handle on the same pixel. Only for single-frame images. Each call rotates what is there now, so turns add up.

```basic
; Turn an image a quarter turn
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
RotateImage ship, 90   ; degrees
DrawImage ship, 100, 100
Flip
```

### TFormImage

`TFormImage image%, a#, b#, c#, d#`

Transforms image by the 2x2 matrix a,b,c,d (for skewing, scaling and rotating).

```basic
; Skew an image sideways with a 2x2 matrix
Graphics 640, 480
tree = LoadImage("tree.png")
TFormImage tree, 1, 0, 0.5, 1   ; a, b, c, d
DrawImage tree, 100, 100
Flip
```

### TFormFilter

`TFormFilter enable%`

Turns smoothing on (1) or off (0) for RotateImage, ScaleImage, ResizeImage and TFormImage.

```basic
; Smooth images when they are rotated or scaled
Graphics 640, 480
TFormFilter 1   ; 1 = smooth, 0 = sharp
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
RotateImage ship, 30
DrawImage ship, 100, 100
Flip
```

### TileImage

`TileImage image%, [x%=0], [y%=0], [frame%=0]`

Fills the whole current buffer with frame of image, offset by x,y, with its mask colour transparent.

```basic
; Fill the whole screen with a repeating pattern
Graphics 640, 480
pattern = LoadImage("checker.png")
TileImage pattern
Flip
```

### TileBlock

`TileBlock image%, [x%=0], [y%=0], [frame%=0]`

Fills the whole current buffer with frame of image, offset by x,y, without transparency.

```basic
; Fill the screen with a pattern that scrolls
Graphics 640, 480
pattern = LoadImage("checker.png")
scroll = 0
While Not KeyHit(1)
    scroll = scroll + 1
    TileBlock pattern, scroll, 0   ; offset x, y
    Flip
Wend
```

### ImageRectCollide

`ImageRectCollide(image%, x%, y%, frame%, rect_x%, rect_y%, rect_width%, rect_height%)` → integer

Returns 1 if any solid (non-mask) pixel of frame of image drawn at x,y lies inside the rectangle at rect_x,rect_y of size rect_width by rect_height (pixel-accurate).

```basic
; Check if the ship touches a rectangle (a door)
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
; image, x, y, frame, then the rectangle: left, top, width, height
If ImageRectCollide(ship, 100, 100, 0, 90, 90, 40, 40) Then Print "Ship touches the door"
```

### ImageWidth

`ImageWidth(image%)` → integer

Returns the width image is drawn at, in pixels: one frame (not a whole animation strip) times any ScaleImage or ResizeImage scale.

```basic
; Centre an image using its width
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
DrawImage ship, (GraphicsWidth() - ImageWidth(ship)) / 2, 100
Flip
```

### ImageHeight

`ImageHeight(image%)` → integer

Returns the height image is drawn at, in pixels: one frame (not a whole animation strip) times any ScaleImage or ResizeImage scale.

```basic
; Put an image on the bottom edge
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
DrawImage ship, 100, GraphicsHeight() - ImageHeight(ship)
Flip
```

### ImageXHandle

`ImageXHandle(image%)` → integer

Returns the X offset of the handle of image in pixels. The handle is the point of the image that sits at the x, y given to DrawImage: 0 (the left edge) unless MidHandle, HandleImage or AutoMidHandle changed it.

```basic
; Print where the handle is horizontally
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
MidHandle ship
Print "Handle x: " + ImageXHandle(ship)
```

### ImageYHandle

`ImageYHandle(image%)` → integer

Returns the Y offset of the handle of image in pixels. The handle is the point of the image that sits at the x, y given to DrawImage: 0 (the top edge) unless MidHandle, HandleImage or AutoMidHandle changed it.

```basic
; Print where the handle is vertically
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
MidHandle ship
Print "Handle y: " + ImageYHandle(ship)
```

### ImageFrames

`ImageFrames(image%)` → integer

Returns the number of frames in image: 1 for an image loaded with LoadImage, and the count of frames for one made with LoadAnimImage.

```basic
; Draw every frame of an animated image side by side
Graphics 640, 480
explosion = LoadAnimImage("explosion.png", 32, 32, 0, 4)
For frame = 0 To ImageFrames(explosion) - 1
    DrawImage explosion, 40 + frame * 40, 100, frame
Next
Flip
```

### HandleImage

`HandleImage image%, x%, y%`

Sets the handle (drawing origin) of image to x,y pixels from its top-left corner.

```basic
; Put the handle at the bottom middle so the image stands on a point
Graphics 640, 480
tree = LoadImage("tree.png")
HandleImage tree, ImageWidth(tree) / 2, ImageHeight(tree)
DrawImage tree, 320, 400   ; the bottom middle of the tree is at 320, 400
Flip
```

### MidHandle

`MidHandle image%`

Moves the handle of image to the centre of one frame, so DrawImage puts the centre of the picture at x, y. Uses the original size, not the ScaleImage size.

```basic
; Put the handle at the centre of the image
Graphics 640, 480
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
MidHandle coin
DrawImage coin, 320, 240   ; the centre of the coin is at 320, 240
Flip
```

### AutoMidHandle

`AutoMidHandle enable%`

When enable is 1, images loaded or created afterwards get their handle at their centre; 0 puts it at the top-left corner.

```basic
; Make every image loaded after this centred
Graphics 640, 480
AutoMidHandle 1
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
ring = LoadImage("ring.png")
DrawImage coin, 200, 200
DrawImage ring, 300, 200
Flip
```

### MaskImage

`MaskImage image%, red%, green%, blue%`

Sets the colour of image (red, green, blue 0-255) that is drawn as transparent. The default is black.

```basic
; Make pure magenta the transparent colour
Graphics 640, 480
sprite = LoadImage("ship.png")
MaskImage sprite, 255, 0, 255   ; red, green, blue
DrawImage sprite, 100, 100
Flip
```

### ScaleImage

`ScaleImage image%, x_scale#, y_scale#`

Sets the size image is drawn at as a multiple of its original size: 2 doubles it, 0.5 halves it. It replaces any earlier scale, and ImageWidth and ImageHeight then report the scaled size.

```basic
; Make an image twice as wide and half as tall
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
ScaleImage ship, 2, 0.5   ; x scale, y scale
DrawImage ship, 100, 100
Flip
```

### ResizeImage

`ResizeImage image%, width#, height#`

Sets the size image is drawn at to width by height pixels, whatever the original size. It replaces any earlier scale, and ImageWidth and ImageHeight then report the new size.

```basic
; Make an image exactly 64 by 64 pixels
Graphics 640, 480
tree = LoadImage("tree.png")
ResizeImage tree, 64, 64
DrawImage tree, 100, 100
Flip
```

### RectsOverlap

`RectsOverlap(x1%, y1%, width1%, height1%, x2%, y2%, width2%, height2%)` → integer

Returns 1 if the rectangle x1,y1,width1,height1 overlaps the rectangle x2,y2,width2,height2.

```basic
; Check if the player box touches the enemy box
playerX = 100 : playerY = 100
enemyX = 120 : enemyY = 110
If RectsOverlap(playerX, playerY, 32, 32, enemyX, enemyY, 32, 32) Then Print "Hit!"
```

### ImagesOverlap

`ImagesOverlap(image1%, x1%, y1%, image2%, x2%, y2%)` → integer

Returns 1 if the bounding rectangles of image1 at x1,y1 and image2 at x2,y2 overlap (fast, not pixel-accurate).

```basic
; Quick check if the boxes of two images overlap
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
If ImagesOverlap(ship, 100, 100, coin, 110, 110) Then Print "Ship is near the coin"
```

### ImagesCollide

`ImagesCollide(image1%, x1%, y1%, frame1%, image2%, x2%, y2%, frame2%)` → integer

Returns 1 if frame1 of image1 at x1,y1 and frame2 of image2 at x2,y2 overlap, ignoring transparent pixels.

```basic
; Pixel accurate check, ignoring transparent parts
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
; image, x, y, frame for each of the two images
If ImagesCollide(ship, 100, 100, 0, coin, 110, 110, 0) Then Print "Ship touched the coin"
```

### ImageRectOverlap

`ImageRectOverlap(image%, x%, y%, rect_x%, rect_y%, rect_width%, rect_height%)` → integer

Returns 1 if the bounding rectangle of image at x,y overlaps the rectangle at rect_x,rect_y of size rect_width by rect_height.

```basic
; Check if the box of the ship overlaps a rectangle
Graphics 640, 480
ship = LoadImage("ship.png")
If ImageRectOverlap(ship, 100, 100, 90, 90, 40, 40) Then Print "Ship is in the zone"
```

### LoadImageAsync

`LoadImageAsync(file$, [width%=0], [height%=0], [first%=0], [count%=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Starts loading an image file in the background and returns its handle at once (a single transparent pixel until it arrives); with width and height it is cut into frames like LoadAnimImage (first and count as there, count 0 = all). Use ImageLoaded or PendingLoads to know when it is ready.

```basic
; Start loading an image in the background, and wait for it
Graphics 640, 480
background = LoadImageAsync("checker.png")
While ImageLoaded(background) = 0
    Cls
    Text 10, 10, "Loading..."
    Flip
Wend
DrawImage background, 0, 0
Flip
```

### ImageLoaded

`ImageLoaded(image%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 when image has loaded (always for other images), 0 while it is loading in the background, -1 if loading failed.

```basic
; 1 = ready, 0 = still loading, -1 = failed
Graphics 640, 480
picture = LoadImageAsync("tree.png")
state = ImageLoaded(picture)
If state = 1 Then Print "Ready"
If state = 0 Then Print "Still loading"
If state = -1 Then Print "Failed"
```

### ImageName

`ImageName(image%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the file image was loaded from, or "" for an image made in the program.

```basic
; Print the file an image came from
Graphics 640, 480
coin = LoadImage("coin.png")
Print ImageName(coin)
```

## Buffers and pixels

### GetColor

`GetColor x%, y%`

Sets the current drawing colour to the colour of the pixel at x, y in the current buffer. Pixels are read from image and texture buffers, not the screen.

```basic
; Read a pixel of an image into the drawing colour
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(16, 16)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(picture)
Color 10, 200, 30
Plot 5, 5
GetColor 5, 5   ; the drawing colour is now the colour of that pixel
Print ColorRed() + ", " + ColorGreen() + ", " + ColorBlue()
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
```

### CopyPixel

`CopyPixel src_x%, src_y%, src_buffer%, dest_x%, dest_y%, [dest_buffer%=0]`

Copies the pixel at src_x, src_y in src_buffer to dest_x, dest_y in dest_buffer (default the current buffer). The source cannot be the screen.

```basic
; Copy one pixel from an image onto the screen
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(16, 16)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(picture)
Color 255, 0, 0
Plot 3, 3
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
CopyPixel 3, 3, ImageBuffer(picture), 100, 100   ; source x, y, source buffer, then destination x, y
Flip
```

### CopyPixelFast

`CopyPixelFast src_x%, src_y%, src_buffer%, dest_x%, dest_y%, [dest_buffer%=0]`

Same as CopyPixel; copies one pixel from src_buffer to dest_buffer.

```basic
; Copy one pixel from one image to another
Graphics 640, 480
source = CreateImage(16, 16)
target = CreateImage(16, 16)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(source)
Color 0, 0, 255
Plot 2, 2
CopyPixelFast 2, 2, ImageBuffer(source), 8, 8, ImageBuffer(target)
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
```

### BufferWidth

`BufferWidth(buffer%)` → integer

Returns the width of buffer in pixels: the graphics window for BackBuffer or FrontBuffer, otherwise the image or texture. buffer comes from BackBuffer, ImageBuffer or TextureBuffer, not an image handle.

```basic
; Print the width of the back buffer
Graphics 640, 480
Print "Width: " + BufferWidth(BackBuffer())
```

### BufferHeight

`BufferHeight(buffer%)` → integer

Returns the height of buffer in pixels: the graphics window for BackBuffer or FrontBuffer, otherwise the image or texture. buffer comes from BackBuffer, ImageBuffer or TextureBuffer, not an image handle.

```basic
; Print the height of an image buffer
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(64, 32)
Print "Height: " + BufferHeight(ImageBuffer(picture))
```

### SaveBuffer

`SaveBuffer(buffer%, file$)` → integer

Saves the contents of buffer (the screen too) to an image file; returns 1 on success, 0 on failure.

```basic
; Take a screenshot
Graphics 640, 480
Text 10, 10, "Screenshot test"
If SaveBuffer(BackBuffer(), "screenshot.png") Then Print "Saved" Else Print "Failed"
```

### LoadBuffer

`LoadBuffer(buffer%, file$)` → integer

Loads an image file into buffer, drawn from the top-left corner (stretched over the whole screen when buffer is the screen); returns 1 on success, 0 on failure.

```basic
; Load a picture onto the back buffer
Graphics 640, 480
If LoadBuffer(BackBuffer(), "checker.png") = 0 Then Print "Could not load"
Flip
```

### BackBuffer

`BackBuffer()` → integer

Returns the back buffer, the hidden screen that is drawn to and shown by Flip.

```basic
; Draw on the hidden screen, then show it with Flip
Graphics 640, 480
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
Text 10, 10, "Drawn on the back buffer"
Flip
```

### FrontBuffer

`FrontBuffer()` → integer

Returns the front buffer, the screen that is currently visible.

```basic
; Draw straight onto the visible screen
Graphics 640, 480
SetBuffer FrontBuffer()
Text 10, 10, "Drawn on the front buffer"
```

### GraphicsBuffer

`GraphicsBuffer()` → integer

Returns the buffer that drawing commands (Text, Rect, DrawImage and so on) draw to at the moment: BackBuffer after Graphics, until SetBuffer changes it. Keep it to switch back later.

```basic
; Remember the current buffer and restore it later
Graphics 640, 480
oldBuffer = GraphicsBuffer()
picture = CreateImage(32, 32)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(picture)
Rect 0, 0, 32, 32
SetBuffer oldBuffer
```

### ImageBuffer

`ImageBuffer(image%, [frame%=0])` → integer

Returns the buffer of frame of image, so it can be drawn to or read.

```basic
; Draw on an image, then draw the image on the screen
Graphics 640, 480
badge = CreateImage(64, 64)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(badge)
Color 255, 200, 0
Oval 0, 0, 64, 64
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
DrawImage badge, 100, 100
Flip
```

### TextureBuffer

`TextureBuffer(texture%, [frame%=0])` → integer

Returns the buffer of frame of texture, so it can be drawn to or read.

```basic
; Paint a red square onto a texture
Graphics3D 800, 600
tex = CreateTexture(64, 64)
SetBuffer TextureBuffer(tex)
Color 255, 0, 0
Rect 0, 0, 64, 64
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
```

### SetBuffer

`SetBuffer buffer%`

Makes buffer the one 2D drawing commands (Text, Rect, DrawImage and so on) draw into: BackBuffer(), FrontBuffer(), ImageBuffer(image) or TextureBuffer(texture). Pass BackBuffer() to draw to the screen again.

```basic
; Draw into an image, then go back to the screen
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(32, 32)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(picture)
Cls
SetBuffer BackBuffer()   ; drawing goes to the screen again
```

### LockBuffer

`LockBuffer [buffer%=0]`

Locks buffer (default the current one) for fast pixel access with ReadPixelFast and WritePixelFast.

```basic
; Lock a buffer while writing many pixels
Graphics 640, 480
LockBuffer BackBuffer()
For x = 0 To 99
    WritePixelFast x, 50, $FFFF0000
Next
UnlockBuffer BackBuffer()
Flip
```

### UnlockBuffer

`UnlockBuffer [buffer%=0]`

Sends changed pixels of image and texture buffers to the graphics card so they show; call it after pixel editing such as WritePixelFast. buffer defaults to the current one. A bad buffer is an error.

```basic
; Unlock the buffer when you have finished with fast pixels
Graphics 640, 480
LockBuffer
WritePixelFast 10, 10, $FF00FF00
UnlockBuffer
Flip
```

### ReadPixel

`ReadPixel(x%, y%, [buffer%=0])` → integer

Returns the colour of the pixel at x, y of buffer as an ARGB value.

```basic
; Read the colour of a pixel and split it into red, green and blue
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(8, 8)
SetBuffer ImageBuffer(picture)
WritePixel 1, 1, $FF3366CC
argb = ReadPixel(1, 1)
red = (argb Shr 16) And 255
green = (argb Shr 8) And 255
blue = argb And 255
Print red + ", " + green + ", " + blue
SetBuffer BackBuffer()
```

### ReadPixelFast

`ReadPixelFast(x%, y%, [buffer%=0])` → integer

Returns the colour of the pixel at x, y of a locked buffer as an ARGB value.

```basic
; Read pixels of a locked buffer
Graphics 640, 480
picture = CreateImage(8, 8)
buffer = ImageBuffer(picture)
LockBuffer buffer
WritePixelFast 2, 2, $FFFFFFFF, buffer
argb = ReadPixelFast(2, 2, buffer)
UnlockBuffer buffer
Print "Pixel: " + Hex(argb)
```

### WritePixel

`WritePixel x%, y%, argb%, [buffer%=0]`

Sets the pixel at x, y of buffer to the ARGB colour argb.

```basic
; Set a pixel with an ARGB value (alpha, red, green, blue)
Graphics 640, 480
WritePixel 100, 100, $FFFF8000   ; solid orange
Flip
```

### WritePixelFast

`WritePixelFast x%, y%, argb%, [buffer%=0]`

Sets the pixel at x, y of a locked buffer to the ARGB colour argb.

```basic
; Fill a row of pixels quickly in a locked buffer
Graphics 640, 480
buffer = BackBuffer()
LockBuffer buffer
For x = 0 To 639
    WritePixelFast x, 240, $FF00FFFF, buffer
Next
UnlockBuffer buffer
Flip
```

### CopyRect

`CopyRect src_x%, src_y%, width%, height%, dest_x%, dest_y%, [src_buffer%=0], [dest_buffer%=0]`

Copies a width by height rectangle at src_x, src_y of src_buffer to dest_x, dest_y of dest_buffer (both default to the current buffer).

```basic
; Copy a 50 by 50 area from one place on the screen to another
Graphics 640, 480
Color 255, 0, 0
Rect 0, 0, 50, 50
CopyRect 0, 0, 50, 50, 200, 100   ; source x, y, width, height, then destination x, y
Flip
```

## Tile maps

### LoadTiledMap

`LoadTiledMap(file$, [level$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** Loads a map from a level editor and returns its handle, or 0 on failure. Reads Tiled (.tmx, .tmj, .world), LDtk (.ldtk), Ogmo Editor 3, Sprite Fusion and Pyxel Edit exports, and tilemap layers in Aseprite files. level picks a level by name in a file that holds several; leave it out for the first. See docs/TOOLS2D.md.

```basic
; a level drawn in the Tiled editor
Graphics 800, 480, 0, 2
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
If map = 0 Then RuntimeError "meadow.tmx not found"
```

### FreeTiledMap

`FreeTiledMap map%`

**(ext)** Frees a Tiled map with its layers, objects and picture.

```basic
; a game with two levels: free each map before loading the next
For level = 1 To 2
    map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
    Print "Level " + level + " has " + CountTiledObjects(map, "coin") + " coins"   ; 8 coins
    FreeTiledMap map                  ; the map, its layers, objects and picture are freed
Next
```

### DrawTiledMap

`DrawTiledMap map%, [x#=0], [y#=0]`

**(ext)** Draws every visible layer of a map in order, with its top-left corner at x, y, using each layer's opacity, tint and parallax. Animated tiles play by themselves. Objects are not drawn; use DrawTiledObject.

```basic
; every visible layer, scrolled by the 2D camera
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Camera2D 480, 240
DrawTiledMap map
```

### CountTiledLevels

`CountTiledLevels(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many levels the file holds that map was loaded from; 1 if it has no levels.

```basic
; an LDtk project or Tiled world can hold several levels
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/forest.ldtk")
Print CountTiledLevels(map)           ; 1
```

### TiledLevelName

`TiledLevelName(map%, index%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of level number index (from 1), to pass to LoadTiledMap.

```basic
; list the levels of a file
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/forest.ldtk")
For i = 1 To CountTiledLevels(map)
    Print TiledLevelName(map, i)      ; Forest
Next
```

### TiledLevelX

`TiledLevelX(map%, index%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns where the editor puts level number index (from 1), in world pixels across.

```basic
; where the editor put the first level
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/forest.ldtk")
Print TiledLevelX(map, 1)
```

### TiledLevelY

`TiledLevelY(map%, index%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns where the editor puts level number index (from 1), in world pixels down.

```basic
; where the editor put the first level
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/forest.ldtk")
Print TiledLevelY(map, 1)
```

### TiledMapLevel

`TiledMapLevel(map%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of the level that map holds, or an empty string if the file has no levels.

```basic
; which level was loaded
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/forest.ldtk", "Forest")
Print TiledMapLevel(map)              ; Forest
```

### TiledMapWidth

`TiledMapWidth(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the width of a map in cells.

```basic
; the size of a map in cells
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledMapWidth(map) + " cells wide"
```

### TiledMapHeight

`TiledMapHeight(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the height of a map in cells.

```basic
; the size of a map in cells
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledMapHeight(map) + " cells high"
```

### TiledMapTileWidth

`TiledMapTileWidth(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the width of a map's grid in pixels.

```basic
; the size of the grid
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledMapTileWidth(map)          ; 32
```

### TiledMapTileHeight

`TiledMapTileHeight(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the height of a map's grid in pixels.

```basic
; the size of the grid
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledMapTileHeight(map)         ; 32
```

### TiledMapPixelWidth

`TiledMapPixelWidth(map%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the width in pixels of the box around a whole map, whatever the shape of its grid.

```basic
; keep the camera inside a map of any shape
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
Print TiledMapPixelWidth(map)         ; 640
```

### TiledMapPixelHeight

`TiledMapPixelHeight(map%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the height in pixels of the box around a whole map, whatever the shape of its grid.

```basic
; keep the camera inside a map of any shape
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
Print TiledMapPixelHeight(map)        ; 320
```

### TiledMapOrientation

`TiledMapOrientation(map%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the shape of a map's grid: "orthogonal", "isometric", "staggered" or "hexagonal".

```basic
; the shape of the grid
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
Print TiledMapOrientation(map)        ; isometric
```

### TiledMapBackground

`TiledMapBackground(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the background colour set in the editor as red * 65536 + green * 256 + blue, or -1 if none was set.

```basic
; clear the screen to the colour set in the editor
Graphics 640, 480, 0, 2
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
c = TiledMapBackground(map)
If c >= 0 Then ClsColor c Shr 16, (c Shr 8) And 255, c And 255
Cls
```

### TiledMapBody2D

`TiledMapBody2D(map%, [layer$=""], [x#=0], [y#=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Makes a static 2D physics body from the collision shapes drawn on tiles in Tiled, for one layer or for all if layer is left out, and returns it. Only tiles with shapes are solid. x, y place the body. Use TileMapBody2D instead to make every tile of a layer a solid cell.

```basic
; solid only where the tileset has collision shapes
Graphics 640, 480, 0, 2
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/extras.tmx")
walls = TiledMapBody2D(map, "Props")
```

### TiledLayerProperty

`TiledLayerProperty(map%, layer$, name$, [fallback$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns a custom property of a tile or picture layer as text, or fallback if it does not have it.

```basic
; a custom property of a layer
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledLayerProperty(map, "Ground", "music", "none")
```

### TiledLayerAlpha

`TiledLayerAlpha map%, layer$, alpha#`

**(ext)** Sets how solid a layer is drawn by DrawTiledMap, from 0 (invisible) to 1.

```basic
; fade a layer
Graphics 640, 480, 0, 2
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/extras.tmx")
TiledLayerAlpha map, "Props", 0.5
```

### TiledImageLayer

`TiledImageLayer(map%, name$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the picture of the layer called name as an image, or 0 if there is no such picture layer.

```basic
; a picture layer as an image
Graphics 640, 480, 0, 2
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/extras.tmx")
hills = TiledImageLayer(map, "Hills")
DrawImage hills, 0, 0
```

### TiledTileType

`TiledTileType(map%, tile%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the type given to a tile in the tileset, from a frame number such as GetTile returns.

```basic
; what kind of tile is at a cell
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/extras.tmx")
props = TiledLayer(map, "Props")
t = GetTile(props, 8, 12)
If t >= 0 Then Print TiledTileType(map, t)   ; hazard
```

### TiledTileProperty

`TiledTileProperty(map%, tile%, name$, [fallback$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns a custom property given to a tile in the tileset as text, from a frame number such as GetTile returns, or fallback if it does not have it.

```basic
; data typed on a tile in the tileset
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/extras.tmx")
props = TiledLayer(map, "Props")
t = GetTile(props, 8, 12)
If t >= 0 Then Print TiledTileProperty(map, t, "damage", "0")   ; 1
```

### TiledMapImage

`TiledMapImage(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the animation image holding every tile of a map. Frames are all the size of the largest tile.

```basic
; the picture holding every tile, for drawing tile objects
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
coin = TiledObject(map, 1, "coin")
DrawImage TiledMapImage(map), TiledObjectX(coin), TiledObjectY(coin), TiledObjectTile(coin)
```

### TiledMapProperty

`TiledMapProperty(map%, name$, [fallback$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns a custom property of a Tiled map as text, or fallback if it does not have it.

```basic
; read the map's own properties, with a fallback for ones the map does not set
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
title$ = TiledMapProperty(map, "title", "Untitled")
music$ = TiledMapProperty(map, "music", "none")
Print title$                          ; Meadow Run
Print music$                          ; none
```

### CountTiledLayers

`CountTiledLayers(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the number of tile layers in map; object layers are not counted. Layers are numbered from 1 in the order of the file (bottom layer first), as TiledLayerName(map, index) uses.

```basic
; how many tile layers the map has
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print CountTiledLayers(map)
```

### TiledLayerName

`TiledLayerName(map%, index%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of tile layer number index (from 1).

```basic
; list the layers
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
For i = 1 To CountTiledLayers(map)
    Print TiledLayerName(map, i)
Next
```

### TiledLayer

`TiledLayer(map%, name$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the layer called name as a tile map for the tile map commands, or 0 if there is none.

```basic
; a layer is a tile map: make the ground solid
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
ground = TiledLayer(map, "Ground")
TileMapBody2D ground
```

### ShowTiledLayer

`ShowTiledLayer map%, name$, [show%=1]`

**(ext)** Shows (show 1, the default) or hides (0) the tile or picture layer called name, so DrawTiledMap draws or skips it. Names ignore case and an unknown name does nothing. The layer stays loaded either way.

```basic
; draw a level, and let the player switch the Decor layer off and on with D
Graphics 960, 480
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
show = True
While Not KeyHit(KEY_ESCAPE)
    If KeyHit(KEY_D) Then show = Not show
    ShowTiledLayer map, "Decor", show
    Cls
    DrawTiledMap map
    Flip
Wend
```

### CountTiledObjects

`CountTiledObjects(map%, [type$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many objects a Tiled map has, or only how many of a type if type is given.

```basic
; how many coins are in the level
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print CountTiledObjects(map, "coin")
```

### TiledObject

`TiledObject(map%, index%, [type$=""])` → integer

**(ext)** Returns object number index (from 1) of a Tiled map, counting as CountTiledObjects does.

```basic
; walk through all the coins of a map and read the value typed on each in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
For i = 1 To CountTiledObjects(map, "coin")
    coin = TiledObject(map, i, "coin")
    Print "coin " + i + " is worth " + TiledObjectProperty(coin, "value")
Next                                  ; the first line is: coin 1 is worth 10
```

### FindTiledObject

`FindTiledObject(map%, name$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the object called name in a Tiled map, or 0 if there is none.

```basic
; find the level's goal by the name given to it in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
goal = FindTiledObject(map, "Goal")
If goal = 0 Then RuntimeError "This level has no Goal"
Print TiledObjectType(goal)           ; goal
Print TiledObjectWidth(goal) + " x " + TiledObjectHeight(goal)   ; 32.0 x 96.0
```

### TiledObjectName

`TiledObjectName(obj%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name given to obj in Tiled, or an empty string if it has none. Many objects can share a name, and FindTiledObject returns only the first one.

```basic
; the name typed in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectName(TiledObject(map, 1, "goal"))
```

### TiledObjectType

`TiledObjectType(obj%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the type (class in some versions of Tiled) of a Tiled object.

```basic
; what kind of object the first one is
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectType(TiledObject(map, 1))
```

### TiledObjectLayer

`TiledObjectLayer(obj%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of the object layer a Tiled object is on.

```basic
; which object layer it is on
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectLayer(TiledObject(map, 1))
```

### TiledObjectX

`TiledObjectX(obj%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the left edge of a Tiled object, in pixels from the left of the map.

```basic
; start the player at the marked place
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
start = TiledObject(map, 1, "spawn")
px# = TiledObjectX(start)
```

### TiledObjectY

`TiledObjectY(obj%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the top edge of a Tiled object, in pixels from the top of the map.

```basic
; start the player at the marked place
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
start = TiledObject(map, 1, "spawn")
py# = TiledObjectY(start)
```

### TiledObjectWidth

`TiledObjectWidth(obj%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the width of obj in pixels as drawn in Tiled. Point objects, such as spawn points, have a width of 0.

```basic
; the size of an area drawn in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectWidth(FindTiledObject(map, "Goal"))
```

### TiledObjectHeight

`TiledObjectHeight(obj%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the height of obj in pixels as drawn in Tiled. Point objects, such as spawn points, have a height of 0.

```basic
; the size of an area drawn in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectHeight(FindTiledObject(map, "Goal"))
```

### TiledObjectRotation

`TiledObjectRotation(obj%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the rotation of a Tiled object in degrees clockwise.

```basic
; how far it was turned in Tiled, in degrees
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectRotation(TiledObject(map, 1))
```

### TiledObjectShape

`TiledObjectShape(obj%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the shape of an object: 0 rectangle, 1 ellipse, 2 point, 3 polygon, 4 polyline, 5 tile, 6 text.

```basic
; 2 is a point
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
If TiledObjectShape(TiledObject(map, 1, "spawn")) = 2 Then Print "a point"
```

### TiledObjectTile

`TiledObjectTile(obj%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the frame of TiledMapImage that a tile object shows, or -1 for other objects.

```basic
; which frame a tile object shows
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectTile(TiledObject(map, 1, "coin"))
```

### TiledObjectText

`TiledObjectText(obj%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the words of a text object, or an empty string for other objects. Its look is in the properties "text.color", "text.pixelsize", "text.fontfamily" and so on.

```basic
; the words of each text object
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
For i = 1 To CountTiledObjects(map)
    If TiledObjectShape(TiledObject(map, i)) = 6 Then Print TiledObjectText(TiledObject(map, i))
Next
```

### DrawTiledObject

`DrawTiledObject obj%, [x#=0], [y#=0]`

**(ext)** Draws a tile object where the editor put it, at its size and rotation, moved by x, y. Other objects draw nothing.

```basic
; tile objects at their place, size and rotation
Graphics 960, 480, 0, 2
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
For i = 1 To CountTiledObjects(map, "coin")
    DrawTiledObject TiledObject(map, i, "coin")
Next
```

### CountTiledObjectPoints

`CountTiledObjectPoints(obj%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many corners a polygon or polyline Tiled object has.

```basic
; a polyline drawn in Tiled makes a patrol route
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
route = TiledObject(map, 1)
Print CountTiledObjectPoints(route)
```

### TiledObjectPointX

`TiledObjectPointX(obj%, index%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the x of corner number index (from 1) of a Tiled object, in map pixels.

```basic
; walk along a polyline drawn in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
route = TiledObject(map, 1)
For i = 1 To CountTiledObjectPoints(route)
    Print TiledObjectPointX(route, i)
Next
```

### TiledObjectPointY

`TiledObjectPointY(obj%, index%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the y of corner number index (from 1) of a Tiled object, in map pixels.

```basic
; walk along a polyline drawn in Tiled
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
route = TiledObject(map, 1)
For i = 1 To CountTiledObjectPoints(route)
    Print TiledObjectPointY(route, i)
Next
```

### TiledObjectProperty

`TiledObjectProperty(obj%, name$, [fallback$=""])` → string

**(ext)** Returns a custom property of a Tiled object as text, or fallback if it does not have it.

```basic
; text typed on an object, or "" if it has none
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectProperty(TiledObject(map, 1, "goal"), "message", "Well done!")
```

### TiledObjectPropertyInt

`TiledObjectPropertyInt(obj%, name$, [fallback%=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Returns a custom property of a Tiled object as a whole number, or fallback if it is missing or not a number.

```basic
; what this coin is worth
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
Print TiledObjectPropertyInt(TiledObject(map, 1, "coin"), "value", 10)
```

### TiledObjectPropertyFloat

`TiledObjectPropertyFloat(obj%, name$, [fallback#=0])` → float

**(ext)** Returns a custom property of a Tiled object as a decimal number, or fallback if it is missing or not a number.

```basic
; a coin carries a value typed in Tiled; use 1.5 when a property is missing
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
coin = TiledObject(map, 1, "coin")
Print TiledObjectPropertyFloat(coin, "value", 1.5)    ; 10.0
Print TiledObjectPropertyFloat(coin, "bounce", 1.5)   ; 1.5
```

### TiledObjectHasProperty

`TiledObjectHasProperty(obj%, name$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 if a Tiled object has the custom property name, otherwise 0.

```basic
; only some coins have a value of their own
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/meadow.tmx")
If TiledObjectHasProperty(TiledObject(map, 1, "coin"), "value") Then Print "valued"
```

### CreateTileMap

`CreateTileMap(image%, width%, height%)` → integer

**(ext)** Creates an empty tile map of width by height tiles, whose tiles are frames of the animation image image, and returns it.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
; a map 20 tiles wide and 15 tall, drawn with the frames of tiles
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
Print "Map size: " + TileMapWidth(map) + " x " + TileMapHeight(map)
```

### LoadTileMap

`LoadTileMap(file$, image%)` → integer

**(ext)** Loads a tile map saved with SaveTileMap from file and draws it with the animation image image (one frame per tile). Returns the tile map handle, or 0 if the file cannot be read.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
; load a map saved earlier; it returns 0 if the file cannot be read
map = LoadTileMap("level1.map", tiles)
If map = 0 Then Print "Could not load level1.map"
```

### SaveTileMap

`SaveTileMap(map%, file$)` → integer

**(ext)** Saves tile map map (its tiles, terrains and autotile rules) to a text file. Returns 1 on success, 0 on failure.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
SetTile map, 3, 4, 1
; returns 1 when the file was written, 0 on failure
If SaveTileMap(map, "level1.map") Then Print "Saved" Else Print "Save failed"
```

### FreeTileMap

`FreeTileMap map%`

**(ext)** Frees map made by CreateTileMap or LoadTileMap; using the handle afterwards is an error. The tile image you gave it is not freed (use FreeImage), and a map from LoadTiledMap is freed with FreeTiledMap instead.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
; ... use the map ...
FreeTileMap map                ; finished with it
```

### SetTile

`SetTile map%, x%, y%, tile%`

**(ext)** Sets the cell at column x, row y of tile map map to frame number tile.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
; put frame 5 in the cell at column 3, row 4 (counting from 0)
SetTile map, 3, 4, 5
```

### GetTile

`GetTile(map%, x%, y%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the frame number in the cell at column x, row y of tile map map.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
SetTile map, 3, 4, 5
frame = GetTile(map, 3, 4)     ; the frame number stored in that cell
Print "Tile at 3,4 is frame " + frame
```

### TileMapWidth

`TileMapWidth(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the width of tile map map in cells (tiles), not pixels. Multiply by the width of one tile to get the size it takes on screen.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
columns = TileMapWidth(map)    ; width in cells
Print "The map has " + columns + " columns"
```

### TileMapHeight

`TileMapHeight(map%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the height of tile map map in cells (tiles), not pixels. Multiply by the height of one tile to get the size it takes on screen.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
rows = TileMapHeight(map)      ; height in cells
Print "The map has " + rows + " rows"
```

### TileMapImage

`TileMapImage map%, image%`

**(ext)** Changes the animation image whose frames tile map map draws.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
; switch the whole map to a different tile set (for example a winter theme)
snowTiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles_snow.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
TileMapImage map, snowTiles
```

### TileMapTerrain

`TileMapTerrain map%, terrain%, first_frame%, [mode%=47]`

**(ext)** Sets up autotiling for terrain number terrain: its tiles start at frame first_frame of the map's image. mode is 16 (four neighbours, 16 frames) or 47 (eight neighbours, 47 "blob" frames, the default).

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
; terrain 1 uses 16 frames starting at frame 0 (mode 16 = four neighbours)
TileMapTerrain map, 1, 0, 16
```

### AutoTile

`AutoTile map%, x%, y%, terrain%`

**(ext)** Paints the cell at column x, row y with terrain number terrain, choosing the frame that matches its neighbours and updating the cells around it.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
TileMapTerrain map, 1, 0, 16
; paint terrain 1 along a row of cells; the frames join up with their neighbours
For column = 2 To 8
    AutoTile map, column, 5, 1
Next
```

### GetTileTerrain

`GetTileTerrain(map%, x%, y%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the terrain number painted with AutoTile in the cell at column x, row y of tile map map.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
TileMapTerrain map, 1, 0, 16
AutoTile map, 4, 4, 1
terrain = GetTileTerrain(map, 4, 4)   ; 1 here; an unpainted cell gives 0
Print "Terrain at 4,4: " + terrain
```

### DrawTileMap

`DrawTileMap map%, [x%=0], [y%=0]`

**(ext)** Draws tile map map with its top-left corner at x,y (pixels, or world coordinates with Camera2D on). Only tiles on screen are drawn.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
Cls
DrawTileMap map, 0, 0          ; top-left corner of the map at 0,0
Flip
WaitKey
```

### TileMapCellX

`TileMapCellX(map%, x#)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the column of tile map map under x, given in pixels from the map's left edge.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
; the map is drawn at 0,0, so the mouse position is also the position on the map
column = TileMapCellX(map, MouseX())
Print "The mouse is over column " + column
```

### TileMapCellY

`TileMapCellY(map%, y#)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the row of tile map map under y, given in pixels from the map's top edge.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
row = TileMapCellY(map, MouseY())   ; the row under the mouse
Print "The mouse is over row " + row
```

### TileAt

`TileAt(map%, x#, y#)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the frame number of the tile under the point x,y, given in pixels from the map's top-left corner.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
; the frame of the tile under pixel 100,70 of the map
frame = TileAt(map, 100, 70)
If frame <> 0 Then Print "Something solid is here"
```

### TileMapPickX

`TileMapPickX(map%, x#, y#)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the column of the cell at x, y in pixels from the map's top-left. On isometric, staggered and hexagonal maps a cell depends on both x and y, so use this rather than TileMapCellX.

```basic
; the cell under a point on a map of any shape
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
ground = TiledLayer(map, "Ground")
Print TileMapPickX(ground, 320, 170)
```

### TileMapPickY

`TileMapPickY(map%, x#, y#)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the row of the cell at x, y in pixels from the map's top-left. On isometric, staggered and hexagonal maps a cell depends on both x and y, so use this rather than TileMapCellY.

```basic
; the cell under a point on a map of any shape
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
ground = TiledLayer(map, "Ground")
Print TileMapPickY(ground, 320, 170)
```

### TileMapCellCenterX

`TileMapCellCenterX(map%, cell_x%, cell_y%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the x of the middle of a cell, in pixels from the map's top-left, on a grid of any shape.

```basic
; stand something in the middle of a cell
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
ground = TiledLayer(map, "Ground")
Print TileMapCellCenterX(ground, 3, 4)
```

### TileMapCellCenterY

`TileMapCellCenterY(map%, cell_x%, cell_y%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the y of the middle of a cell, in pixels from the map's top-left, on a grid of any shape.

```basic
; stand something in the middle of a cell
map = LoadTiledMap("examples/media/tiled/iso.tmx")
ground = TiledLayer(map, "Ground")
Print TileMapCellCenterY(ground, 3, 4)
```

## 2D camera

### Camera2D

`Camera2D x#, y#, [zoom#=1], [rotation#=0]`

**(ext)** Turns on the 2D camera: 2D drawing then uses world coordinates, with world point x,y at the centre of the screen, zoom scaling the view (2 = twice as big) and rotation turning it clockwise in degrees.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
player = LoadImage("ship.png")
Cls
; world point 500,300 in the middle of the screen, zoomed in 2x, not rotated
Camera2D 500, 300, 2, 0
DrawImage player, 500, 300
Flip
WaitKey
```

### Camera2DEnable

`Camera2DEnable enable%`

**(ext)** Turns the 2D camera on (1) or off (0), for example to draw a HUD in screen coordinates on top.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Camera2D 500, 300, 2
Cls
Text 500, 300, "World text"      ; drawn in world coordinates
Camera2DEnable 0                 ; off: back to screen pixels for the HUD
Text 10, 10, "Score: 100"
Camera2DEnable 1                 ; on again for the next frame
Flip
WaitKey
```

### ScreenToWorld2DX

`ScreenToWorld2DX(x#, y#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the world X coordinate under screen point x,y for the 2D camera.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Camera2D 500, 300, 2
; where in the world is the mouse pointing?
worldX# = ScreenToWorld2DX(MouseX(), MouseY())
Print "Mouse is at world X " + worldX#
```

### ScreenToWorld2DY

`ScreenToWorld2DY(x#, y#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the world Y coordinate under screen point x,y for the 2D camera.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Camera2D 500, 300, 2
worldY# = ScreenToWorld2DY(MouseX(), MouseY())
Print "Mouse is at world Y " + worldY#
```

### WorldToScreen2DX

`WorldToScreen2DX(x#, y#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the screen X coordinate where world point x,y appears with the 2D camera.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Camera2D 500, 300, 2
; the screen X where the world point 520,300 appears
screenX# = WorldToScreen2DX(520, 300)
Print "Draw the label at screen X " + screenX#
```

### WorldToScreen2DY

`WorldToScreen2DY(x#, y#)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the screen Y coordinate where world point x,y appears with the 2D camera.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Camera2D 500, 300, 2
screenY# = WorldToScreen2DY(520, 300)
Print "Draw the label at screen Y " + screenY#
```

## 2D animation

### LoadSheetImage

`LoadSheetImage(file$)` → integer

**(ext)** Loads a sprite sheet from the .json file written by Aseprite or TexturePacker as an animation image of equal frames, keeping its tags and frame times. Returns the image, or 0 on failure. See docs/TOOLS2D.md.

```basic
; a character from Aseprite: trimmed frames come back the same size
Graphics 800, 480, 0, 2
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
MidHandle hero
DrawImage hero, 100, 100, 0
```

### CountSheetTags

`CountSheetTags(image%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many named animation tags the sprite sheet image has (from Aseprite or TexturePacker), or 0 if it has none or was not loaded with LoadSheetImage.

```basic
; how many named animations the sheet has
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
Print CountSheetTags(hero)
```

### SheetTagName

`SheetTagName(image%, index%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of tag number index (from 1) of a sprite sheet image.

```basic
; list the named animations
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
For i = 1 To CountSheetTags(hero)
    Print SheetTagName(hero, i)
Next
```

### SheetTagFirst

`SheetTagFirst(image%, tag$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the first frame number of the tag called tag in sprite sheet image (names ignore capitals). It is an error if the tag does not exist. Use it with SheetTagLast to set an animation's frames.

```basic
; play the "run" tag of a sprite sheet: its first and last frame set the animation
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
startFrame = SheetTagFirst(hero, "run")
endFrame = SheetTagLast(hero, "run")
run = CreateAnim2D(hero, startFrame, endFrame, 12)
Print "Run uses frames " + startFrame + " to " + endFrame
```

### SheetTagLast

`SheetTagLast(image%, tag$)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the last frame number of the tag called tag in sprite sheet image (names ignore capitals). It is an error if the tag does not exist. Use it with SheetTagFirst to set an animation's frames.

```basic
; how long a tag is
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
Print SheetTagLast(hero, "run") - SheetTagFirst(hero, "run") + 1
```

### SheetFrameTime

`SheetFrameTime(image%, frame%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many milliseconds a frame of a sprite sheet shows, or 0 for other images.

```basic
; how many milliseconds a frame shows
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
Print SheetFrameTime(hero, 0)
```

### Anim2DPlayTag

`Anim2DPlayTag anim%, tag$, [mode%=0]`

**(ext)** Plays a tag of a sprite sheet with its own frame times and direction, or does nothing if that tag is already playing. mode is 0 for the tag's own direction, 1 loop, 2 ping-pong or 3 play once.

```basic
; safe to ask every frame: it only restarts when the tag changes
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
anim = CreateAnim2D(hero)
Anim2DPlayTag anim, "run"
DrawImage hero, 100, 100, Anim2DFrame(anim)
```

### Anim2DTag

`Anim2DTag(anim%)` → string

**(ext)** Returns the name of the tag anim is playing, or "" if none.

```basic
; which tag is playing
hero = LoadSheetImage("examples/media/tiled/hero.json")
anim = CreateAnim2D(hero)
Anim2DPlayTag anim, "idle"
Print Anim2DTag(anim)
```

### CreateAnim2D

`CreateAnim2D(image%, [first%=0], [last%=-1], [fps#=10], [mode%=1])` → integer

**(ext)** Creates an animation that plays frames first to last (-1 = last frame) of the animation image image at fps frames a second. mode is 1 loop, 2 ping-pong, 3 play once. Returns the animation handle.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
; frames 0 to 3 at 10 frames a second; mode 1 = loop (2 ping-pong, 3 play once)
walk = CreateAnim2D(frames, 0, 3, 10, 1)
```

### Anim2DPlay

`Anim2DPlay anim%, first%, last%, [fps#=10], [mode%=1]`

**(ext)** Starts anim playing frames first to last at fps frames a second, with mode 1 loop, 2 ping-pong or 3 play once.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
anim = CreateAnim2D(frames, 0, 3, 10, 1)
; switch to frames 4 to 7 at 12 frames a second, playing once (mode 3)
Anim2DPlay anim, 4, 7, 12, 3
```

### Anim2DPause

`Anim2DPause anim%, [pause%=1]`

**(ext)** Pauses anim (pause 1) or resumes it (pause 0). While paused it keeps showing the same frame, and resuming continues from there rather than restarting.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
anim = CreateAnim2D(frames, 0, 3, 10)
Anim2DPause anim, 1            ; 1 = pause
Anim2DPause anim, 0            ; 0 = resume
```

### Anim2DFrame

`Anim2DFrame(anim%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns the frame number of anim's image that is showing now, in the image's own numbering (between the first and last frame you gave CreateAnim2D). It changes with the clock.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
anim = CreateAnim2D(frames, 0, 3, 10)
current = Anim2DFrame(anim)    ; the frame number showing right now
Print "Showing frame " + current
```

### Anim2DPlaying

`Anim2DPlaying(anim%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns 1 while anim is playing, 0 when paused or finished.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
anim = CreateAnim2D(frames, 4, 7, 12, 3)   ; plays once
If Anim2DPlaying(anim) = 0 Then Print "Finished" Else Print "Still playing"
```

### DrawAnim2D

`DrawAnim2D anim%, x#, y#`

**(ext)** Draws the current frame of anim at x,y like DrawImage.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
anim = CreateAnim2D(frames, 0, 3, 10)
While Not KeyHit(1)
    Cls
    DrawAnim2D anim, 320, 240      ; draws the current frame, like DrawImage
    Flip
Wend
```

### FreeAnim2D

`FreeAnim2D anim%`

**(ext)** Frees anim so its handle is no longer valid. The image it showed is not freed and can be used by other animations or drawn with DrawImage.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
frames = LoadAnimImage("hero.png", 32, 32, 0, 8)
anim = CreateAnim2D(frames, 0, 3, 10)
FreeAnim2D anim                ; frees the animation, not the image
FreeImage frames
```

## 2D particles

### CreateParticles2D

`CreateParticles2D([image%=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Creates a 2D particle system that throws out copies of image (default a soft white dot) and returns its handle: sparks, smoke, dust, rain and explosions for 2D games. Draw it every frame with DrawParticles2D.

```basic
; make a spark system, give it a position and shoot 30 particles when something is hit
Graphics3D 800, 600
sparks = CreateParticles2D()
PositionParticles2D sparks, 400, 300
EmitParticles2D sparks, 30
While Not KeyHit(KEY_ESCAPE)
    Cls
    DrawParticles2D sparks
    Flip
Wend
```

### PositionParticles2D

`PositionParticles2D particles%, x#, y#`

**(ext)** Sets where new particles appear, in 2D drawing coordinates. Particles already alive keep flying where they are, so move the system every frame to leave a trail.

```basic
; a campfire at the bottom middle of the screen, burning continuously
Graphics 640, 480
fire = CreateParticles2D()
PositionParticles2D fire, 320, 400
Particles2DRate fire, 40
While Not KeyHit(KEY_ESCAPE)
    Cls
    DrawParticles2D fire
    Flip
Wend
```

### Particles2DRate

`Particles2DRate particles%, per_second#`

**(ext)** Makes the system emit per_second particles steadily (0, the default, emits only with EmitParticles2D).

```basic
smoke = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DRate smoke, 30          ; 30 a second, all the time
```

### EmitParticles2D

`EmitParticles2D particles%, count%`

**(ext)** Throws out count particles at once from the system's position: an explosion or a burst of sparks.

```basic
; an explosion: 200 sparks at once where the ship was
boom = CreateParticles2D()
PositionParticles2D boom, 200, 150
EmitParticles2D boom, 200
```

### Particles2DLife

`Particles2DLife particles%, min#, max#`

**(ext)** Sets how long particles live, between min and max seconds.

```basic
; short-lived dust puffs, lasting between 0.3 and 0.8 seconds
dust = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DLife dust, 0.3, 0.8
EmitParticles2D dust, 20
Print Particles2DCount(dust)        ; 20
```

### Particles2DSpeed

`Particles2DSpeed particles%, min#, max#, [angle#=-90], [spread#=360]`

**(ext)** Sets how fast particles start, between min and max pixels a second, towards angle degrees (0 right, 90 down, -90 up, the default) spread over spread degrees (360, the default, is every way).

```basic
; a fountain: fast, upwards, in a narrow spray (-90 is up, spread of 20 degrees)
fountain = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DSpeed fountain, 200, 300, -90, 20
Particles2DGravity fountain, 0, 400
EmitParticles2D fountain, 100
Print Particles2DCount(fountain)        ; 100
```

### Particles2DGravity

`Particles2DGravity particles%, x#, y#`

**(ext)** Sets the pull on particles in pixels a second per second: 0, 200 falls down, 0, -50 rises like smoke.

```basic
fountain = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DGravity fountain, 0, 400        ; the water falls back down
```

### Particles2DSize

`Particles2DSize particles%, start#, end#`

**(ext)** Sets the particles' scale at birth and at death (1 is the image's own size).

```basic
smoke = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DSize smoke, 0.5, 3              ; puffs grow as they rise
```

### Particles2DSpin

`Particles2DSpin particles%, min#, max#`

**(ext)** Sets how fast particles turn, between min and max degrees a second.

```basic
; falling leaves that twirl either way, up to 90 degrees a second
leaves = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DSpin leaves, -90, 90
Particles2DGravity leaves, 0, 30
EmitParticles2D leaves, 10
Print Particles2DCount(leaves)        ; 10
```

### Particles2DColor

`Particles2DColor particles%, red#, green#, blue#, [alpha#=1]`

**(ext)** Sets the particles' colour and alpha (0-1) at birth; they change smoothly to the fade colour.

```basic
fire = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DColor fire, 255, 220, 80, 1     ; bright yellow ...
```

### Particles2DFadeColor

`Particles2DFadeColor particles%, red#, green#, blue#, [alpha#=0]`

**(ext)** Sets the particles' colour and alpha at death (default the same colour, alpha 0: they fade out).

```basic
fire = CreateParticles2D()
Particles2DFadeColor fire, 200, 30, 0, 0   ; ... to a fading red
```

### DrawParticles2D

`DrawParticles2D particles%`

**(ext)** Moves the particles on by the time since the last call and draws them. Call it once a frame.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
rain = CreateParticles2D()
PositionParticles2D rain, 320, -10
Particles2DRate rain, 200
Particles2DSpeed rain, 300, 400, 90, 10
While Not KeyHit(KEY_ESCAPE)
    Cls
    DrawParticles2D rain
    Flip
Wend
```

### Particles2DCount

`Particles2DCount(particles%)` → integer

**(ext)** Returns how many particles of the system are alive right now. Emitted particles count at once; ones that have run out of life are removed when you call DrawParticles2D.

```basic
sparks = CreateParticles2D()
EmitParticles2D sparks, 50
Print Particles2DCount(sparks)
```

### FreeParticles2D

`FreeParticles2D particles%`

**(ext)** Deletes a 2D particle system and all its living particles. Its handle is invalid afterwards. The image it used is not freed.

```basic
; free an explosion once it has finished, so it does not pile up
boom = CreateParticles2D()
EmitParticles2D boom, 80
FreeParticles2D boom
boom = 0
```

## 2D physics

### Physics2DGravity

`Physics2DGravity x#, y#`

**(ext)** Sets 2D physics gravity to x,y in pixels per second squared (Y down) and starts the 2D physics world.

```basic
; a ball dropping onto a floor under 600 pixels per second squared of gravity
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
floor = CreateBody2D(1, 320, 460) : Body2DBox floor, 640, 40
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 50) : Body2DCircle ball, 16
While Not KeyHit(1)
    UpdateWorld
    Cls
    Oval Body2DX(ball) - 16, Body2DY(ball) - 16, 32, 32
    Flip
Wend
```

### Physics2DScale

`Physics2DScale pixels_per_metre#`

**(ext)** Sets how many pixels make one physics metre (default 50). Set it before creating bodies.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DScale 100             ; 100 pixels = 1 physics metre (set before making bodies)
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
```

### CreateBody2D

`CreateBody2D([type%=0], [x#=0], [y#=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Creates a 2D physics body at x,y pixels. type is 0 dynamic, 1 static or 2 kinematic. Returns the body handle; give it shapes with Body2DBox and similar commands.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
; type 0 = dynamic, 1 = static, 2 = kinematic; starts at 320,100
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DCircle ball, 16
```

### FreeBody2D

`FreeBody2D body%`

**(ext)** Frees 2D body body, its shapes and its place in the physics world. The handle is invalid afterwards. An image you drew with it is untouched.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
crate = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DBox crate, 32, 32
FreeBody2D crate               ; the body and its shapes are gone
```

### Body2DBox

`Body2DBox body%, width#, height#, [offset_x#=0], [offset_y#=0]`

**(ext)** Adds a box shape width by height pixels to body, centred at offset_x,offset_y from the body.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ground = CreateBody2D(1, 320, 460)    ; static
Body2DBox ground, 640, 40             ; 640 wide, 40 tall
crate = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DBox crate, 32, 32, 0, 0         ; last two: offset from the body centre
```

### Body2DCircle

`Body2DCircle body%, radius#, [offset_x#=0], [offset_y#=0]`

**(ext)** Adds a circle shape of radius pixels to body, centred at offset_x,offset_y from the body.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DCircle ball, 16          ; radius 16 pixels
```

### Body2DCapsule

`Body2DCapsule body%, width#, height#, [offset_x#=0], [offset_y#=0]`

**(ext)** Adds an upright capsule (pill) shape width by height pixels to body, centred at offset_x,offset_y; good for characters.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
hero = CreateBody2D(0, 100, 300)
Body2DCapsule hero, 20, 40     ; 20 wide, 40 tall pill shape
Body2DFixedRotation hero, 1    ; stay upright
```

### Body2DPolygon

`Body2DPolygon body%, points$`

**(ext)** Adds a polygon shape to body from points, a string "x1,y1,x2,y2,..." of at least three points in pixels relative to the body.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ramp = CreateBody2D(1, 300, 400)
; three corners (x,y pairs) relative to the body: a triangle
Body2DPolygon ramp, "-60,20,60,20,60,-20"
```

### Body2DSegment

`Body2DSegment body%, x1#, y1#, x2#, y2#`

**(ext)** Adds a line segment shape from x1,y1 to x2,y2 (pixels, relative to the body) to body.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
wall = CreateBody2D(1, 0, 0)
; a thin line from 0,450 to 640,450 acting as the ground
Body2DSegment wall, 0, 450, 640, 450
```

### TileMapBody2D

`TileMapBody2D(map%, [x#=0], [y#=0])` → integer

**(ext)** Creates a static body with boxes for the solid (non-empty) tiles of tile map map, with the map's top-left corner at x,y. Returns the body handle.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
tiles = LoadAnimImage("tiles.png", 32, 32, 0, 16)
map = CreateTileMap(tiles, 20, 15)
For column = 0 To 19
    SetTile map, column, 14, 1       ; a floor of solid tiles
Next
; one static body with a box for every non-empty tile, map corner at 0,0
level = TileMapBody2D(map, 0, 0)
```

### Body2DFriction

`Body2DFriction body%, friction#`

**(ext)** Sets how much body's shapes grip what they touch: 0 is slippery like ice, 0.5 is the default and higher grips more. It applies to shapes already added and ones added later.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
crate = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DBox crate, 32, 32
Body2DFriction crate, 0.1      ; low friction: slippery like ice
```

### Body2DBounce

`Body2DBounce body%, bounce#`

**(ext)** Sets the bounciness (restitution) of body's shapes (0 = no bounce, 1 = full bounce).

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DCircle ball, 16
Body2DBounce ball, 0.8         ; 0 = no bounce, 1 = full bounce
```

### Body2DDensity

`Body2DDensity body%, density#`

**(ext)** Sets the density of body's shapes, which sets its mass.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
crate = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DBox crate, 32, 32
Body2DDensity crate, 5         ; heavier than the default
```

### Body2DSensor

`Body2DSensor body%, enable%`

**(ext)** Makes body's shapes sensors (enable 1) that detect overlaps with Body2DTouching but do not collide.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
coin = CreateBody2D(1, 400, 300)
Body2DCircle coin, 12
Body2DSensor coin, 1           ; detects overlaps but does not block
hero = CreateBody2D(0, 100, 300)
Body2DCapsule hero, 20, 40
If Body2DTouching(hero, coin) Then Print "Picked up the coin"
```

### Body2DFixedRotation

`Body2DFixedRotation body%, enable%`

**(ext)** Stops body from rotating (enable 1) or lets it rotate again (0).

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
hero = CreateBody2D(0, 100, 300)
Body2DCapsule hero, 20, 40
Body2DFixedRotation hero, 1    ; 1 = never rotates, 0 = rotates again
```

### Body2DGravityScale

`Body2DGravityScale body%, scale#`

**(ext)** Multiplies the gravity on body by scale (0 = no gravity).

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
balloon = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 400)
Body2DCircle balloon, 20
Body2DGravityScale balloon, -0.2   ; negative: floats upwards
```

### Body2DDamping

`Body2DDamping body%, linear#, [angular#=0]`

**(ext)** Sets the linear and angular damping of body, which slow its movement and spin.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DCircle ball, 16
Body2DDamping ball, 0.5, 1.0   ; slows movement (0.5) and spin (1.0)
```

### PositionBody2D

`PositionBody2D body%, x#, y#`

**(ext)** Moves body straight to x, y in pixels (Y down) without simulating the trip, so it jumps past anything in between. Use it to place or respawn a body, not to walk it.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
hero = CreateBody2D(0, 100, 300)
Body2DCapsule hero, 20, 40
PositionBody2D hero, 100, 300  ; teleport back to the start (pixels)
```

### RotateBody2D

`RotateBody2D body%, angle#`

**(ext)** Sets body's angle to angle degrees clockwise (0 is unrotated), turning it at once. Use SetSpin2D instead to make it rotate continuously.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
crate = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DBox crate, 32, 32
RotateBody2D crate, 45         ; tilted 45 degrees clockwise
```

### Body2DX

`Body2DX(body%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the X position of body's centre in pixels, measured from the left of the screen. It changes as the physics world moves the body.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DCircle ball, 16
x# = Body2DX(ball)             ; position in pixels
Print "Ball X: " + x#
```

### Body2DY

`Body2DY(body%)` → float

**(ext)** Returns the Y position of body's centre in pixels, measured from the top of the screen (Y points down). It changes as the physics world moves the body.

```basic
Graphics 640, 480
Physics2DGravity 0, 600
ball = CreateBody2D(0, 320, 100)
Body2DCircle ball, 16
y# = Body2DY(ball)
Print "Ball Y: " + y#
```
